#### Library paths and evidence (CR, CX)

- **CR-01.** Library: go1.27.1 regexp/syntax/parse.go:1599-1615 parseNamedClass: strings.Index(s[2:], ":]") scans to the end of the whole expression and returns nil without error when absent, so parseClass (:1836, the '[:' check before parseUnicodeClass) takes '[' as a literal and repeats the scan at the next '[:' (quadratic in the expression). Parses per pattern: patternsize.go:163 (walk), :105 and :108 (compilePattern: syntax.Parse, then regexp.Compile parses again), regexcost.go:289 (patternCosts.of). Evidence: Verifier probe \<D>=/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-ruralz/758d7ca3-81cf-5fbc-a967-12d97bce3d6b/scratchpad/wp51audit/verify-cross, zz_vc_posix_test.go (build: CGO_ENABLED=0 go test -c -o \<D>/probe.test -overlay \<D>/overlay.json ./internal/filter/validation/jsonschema/, run from the package dir with -test.run TestVCPosix). expr = "[" + "[:"\*n + "a]": syntax.Parse n=10,000 11.5 ms, n=40,000 175 ms, n=130,000 (260,003 bytes) 2.02 s, scan {escapes:0 folded:0}. CompileSchema of {"pattern":expr} (260,017 bytes, within MaxSchemaBytes) returns nil after 7.80 s; as a patternProperties name 7.92 s. Without the closing ']' the pattern is refused, but only after 1.97 s (the walk's own parse). Under $recursiveRef (no walk) accepted in 6.0 s (TestVCRecursiveRef). Exceeds 07 F6 (1 s) about 8x, uncancellable in Check and Factory.Build.
- **CR-02.** Library: parse.go:1760-1834 parseUnicodeClass: plain escapes appendTable/appendNegatedTable (:2088, :2113, strided ranges expanded one per rune); under FoldCase it appends tab and its fold table to tmpClass, sorts and merges them (cleanClass :1932), then appendClass; unicodeTable :1720. Evidence: Verifier probes zz_vc_fold_test.go and zz_vc_fold2_test.go (go1.27.1): (?i)\p{Ll} appends 1,336 ranges (Ll plus FoldCategory Ll, strided expanded) and is charged 143 (9.3x); \p{Lu} 1,326 vs 176 (7.5x); Lt 25 vs 9; all scripts at most 1.5x. Plain escapes are exact. A document of 8 patternProperties (?i)[\p{Ll}x224]i (measure 16,077 each, accepted, 16 KB) compiles in 550 ms; (?i)[\p{Lu}x180] 455 ms; the benchmarked shape of BenchmarkCompileAnalysisWorstCase 'combined', (?i)[\pLx45], 105 ms. So the uncharged work is 5.2x the benchmarked worst and adds about 0.45 s to the combined worst case (already 0.95-1.2 s against F6's 1 s).
- **CR-03.** Library: parse.go:2020-2053 appendFoldedRange (SimpleFold orbit per rune in [minFold,maxFold] unless the range covers it all), parseClass :1915-1919. Evidence: Mutant m03 (foldWork returns 1, patternsize.go:546) is killed by TestPatternProgramLimits and TestPatternParsesBounded (\<D>/mutate.py, muts1.json, muts1.out). The scan mirrors the parser's full-range and out-of-range shortcuts (parse.go:2022-2029 vs patternsize.go:543-545).
- **CR-04.** Library: parse.go:1585-1597 parsePerlClassEscape, :1599-1615 parseNamedClass, :1617-1640 appendGroup -> appendFoldedClass (:2063) then cleanClass. Evidence: Mutant m04 (const asciiFold = 0) survives TestPatternProgramLimits, TestScanPatternPrefixes and TestPatternParsesBounded (muts1.out). Without the charge a class merging thousands of (?i)[[:alpha:]...] or (?i)[\d\d...] groups is one node of few ranges (measure about 28) while the parser folds up to 128 runes per group, so a 256 KiB pattern of about 29,000 groups would be accepted; with it, at most about 1,000 groups per pattern. The bound value is right (ASCII groups fold at most 128 runes).
- **CR-05.** Library: parse.go:549-583 collapse, :589-776 factor (rounds 1-4), :790-825 removeLeadingString (copies the remaining runes of every alternative of a run per level), :844 removeLeadingRegexp; depth bounded by maxHeight (parse.go:94, checkHeight :258) only after the factoring recursion has run. Evidence: Auditor probe: a|aa|...|a^499 (125,248 bytes) parses in 18 ms, measure 3,016, accepted (4 parses about 75 ms); n=520 refused by Go after 25 ms. Verifier code reading: the work is about n^3/6 rune copies for n prefix-nested alternatives; shapes with long tails (n^2\*T/2 copies) are refused by the measure after one parse (at most about 50 ms at 256 KiB), accepted shapes stay under about 1M copies.
- **CR-06.** Library: parse.go:404-450 repeat, :452-475 repeatIsValid, :170-256 checkSize/calcSize, :103 maxSize, :123 maxRunes, :1102 parseRepeat (counts over 1000 refused). Evidence: Mutant m39 (OpRepeat size = sub, patternsize.go:234) is killed by TestProgramSizeBound and TestPatternProgramLimits (muts2.out). Go refuses nesting products over 1000 before any expansion.
- **CR-07.** Library: parse.go:258-291 checkHeight (maxHeight 1000, only once 1000 nodes exist), simplify.go:14-120 (x{0,m} adds m nested quests, m<=1000), compile.go:89-159, regexp.go:267-304 minInputLen. Evidence: Code reading (auditor, confirmed): every tree Go accepts is at most 1000 high and Simplify adds at most about 2,000 levels per path (product of nested counts <= 1000), so recursion stays a few thousand frames of under 1 KB; no WP-51 test exercises it.
- **CR-08.** Library: regexp.go:166-212 compile -> parse.go:258-291 checkHeight (ErrNestingDepth). Evidence: Verifier probe TestVCHeight (zz_vc_regex_test.go): "("x999+"a"+")"x999: regexp.Compile ok, walk 0 issues, compilePattern fails 'expression nests too deeply', CompileSchema refuses with the meta-schema's format message "invalid JSON Schema: '((((...' ... at /pattern"; 998 deep passes; 999 non-capturing groups pass (they add no node); 1000 deep refused by both. A valid RE2 pattern is refused with a misleading message.
- **CR-09.** Library: parse.go:141-160 newRegexp/reuse, simplify.go:62-118 (x{n,m}: n copies plus m-n nested Concat+Quest nodes), compile.go:161-165 inst append (old and new arrays live while it grows). Evidence: Code reading: x{0,1000} builds about 2,000 Regexp nodes (about 224 KB) for 2,000 instructions measured at 80 KB, transient; never measured.
- **CR-10.** Library: compile.go:71-296 Compile (one Inst per literal rune, class, alternation, quest/star/plus, capture, empty-width). Evidence: Mutants killed: m11 (literal size 1), m39 (repeat), m40b (nodes dropped), m41 (programOverhead 0), m45 (class ranges 0) by TestProgramSizeBound, TestPatternProgramLimits and TestPatternProgramMemory (muts1-3.out). Which patterns get measured is CX-27 ($recursiveRef targets escape it).
- **CR-11.** Library: compile.go:164 c.p.Inst = append(...) keeps append's spare capacity (cap 512 at 313 instructions); regexp.go:197-205 prefix string and prefixBytes (two copies of an unanchored literal prefix, up to 4 bytes per rune each); parse.go:339-366 maybeConcat grows the literal's rune slice the program points into. Evidence: Verifier probes TestVCKept and TestVCKeptDocument (zz_vc_regex_test.go): per pattern X+U+1F600{309} keeps 27,903 B against a measure of 340 (13,600 B), 2.05x; the anchored run 2.18x; ASCII run of 259 1.39x. fillPatterns documents: U+1F600{309} (385 patterns, 12,611-byte schema) keeps 11,395,536 B; anchored 10,337,592; {316} 11,166,096; ASCII a{259} 6,917,280; against doc.go's 6 MB and the test's 8 MiB.
- **CR-12.** Library: onepass.go:459-467 compileOnePass returns nil unless the program starts with the EmptyBeginText instruction; otherwise onePassCopy/makeOnePass build tables quadratic in alternations. Evidence: Mutant m05 (noOnePass = "") is killed by TestPatternProgramMemory and TestCompilePatternMatches (muts1.out). syntax.Prog.StartCond and Prefix skip the Nop (prog.go skipNop), so anchoring and prefix search are unchanged.
- **CR-13.** Library: backtrack.go:80-114 reset (visited made with capacity maxBacktrackVector/visitedBits uint32 = 32 KiB), :116-125 shouldVisit. Evidence: Mutant m01 (matcherBytes 0) is killed only by TestHandlePatternFit (the 20,000/21,000-byte thresholds of ^[^<>]\*$ at 64 KiB); TestHandleMatcherCharge compares against the same constant and survives. The visited slice is always allocated at full capacity, equal to the 32 KiB term.
- **CR-14.** Library: backtrack.go:129-135 push, :138-302 tryBacktrack (InstAlt reminder; InstCapture pushes nothing with ncap 0), :305-365 backtrack (unanchored loop), job 16 bytes. Evidence: Mutants killed: m09 (held 0) by all four; m38 (jobBytes 16) by TestHandleBacktrackCharge; m43 (heldBudget check off) by TestHeldAlternations (muts1/2.out). TestHandleBacktrackCharge samples the heap (CX-13's class).
- **CR-15.** Library: regexp.go:224 matchSize {128,512,2048,16384,0}; :207-212 mpool picks the first bucket >= len(prog.Inst); :231-257 get allocates q0 and q1 of the bucket size (4+16 bytes per slot each; len(Inst) in the large pool); exec.go:104-118 alloc (thread 32 B + cap slice per rune instruction alive), :246-254 clear (pool slice). Evidence: Verifier probe TestVCPoolsTogether2 (zz_vc_combo2_test.go) through Factory.Build and Handle: (?:a{1000}a{1000}a{50})?b (2,055 instructions, bucket 16384) on a 10-byte string at maxRequestBodyBytes 64 KiB: reserved 205,851, peak 655,360 (3.2x, +449,509); at the default +465,893. TotalAlloc of one match after clearing the pools (TestVCNFAMemory): 2,054 instructions 802,832 B vs 164,224 charged (4.89x); 2,103 4.81x; a distinct alternation of 2,403 instructions 3.82x; 518 1.77x; 16,004 1.89x; 134 0.83x.
- **CR-16.** Library: exec.go:317-376 add recurses on InstAlt.Out (:340) and loops on Arg; compile.go:201-212 alt puts the previous alternation in Out, so an n-way alternation recurses n-1 deep; compile.go:219-251 quest/loop put the continuation in Out when non-greedy; the NFA runs every program over 500 instructions and every input past maxBitStateLen (regexp.go find, backtrack.go:73-75). Evidence: Verifier probe TestVCStackOne (zz_vc_stack_test.go, one fresh process per case, StackInuse growth): plain alternation of 2,700 distinct two-rune literals (measure 16,223, accepted) 524,288 B, anchored the same, 1,000 branches 262,144; (?:a??)x1000 229,376; (?:a??){1000}x8 (16,048) 2,064,384; (?U)(?:a\*)x4000 1,015,808 (refused by the measure, smaller counts are not); greedy control 0. Charged transients 160,960 to 1,056,960 B with no stack term. Growth doubles the goroutine stack and copies it, on top of the validation stack, so the excess can reach the whole stack size. Inputs shorter than the pattern's minInputLen never reach add.
- **CR-17.** Library: exec.go:175-243 match, :260-310 step, :317 add; backtrack.go:138-302 (each (pc,pos) once, an alternation twice), :89-95 visited clearing; prog.go MatchRunePos (fold orbit <= 4, <= 4 pairs scanned, binary search beyond). Evidence: Mutant m10 (queueBound returns 1) is killed by TestQueueBoundCoversInputs; m36 (pattern perByte 0) and m37 (fixed 0) by TestWorkCharges (muts1/2.out). Code reading: the backtracker's pcs at one position are a subset of the NFA queue there (visited never cleared between start positions).
- **CR-18.** Library: regexp.go:231-257 get (zeroes two queues of the bucket, 655 KB for any program of 2,049-16,384 instructions), exec.go:104-118 thread allocations, backtrack.go:49-55 and :80-96 (bitState, 32 KiB visited); amortized by sync.Pool until a collection clears it. Evidence: Code reading: zeroing 655 KB of queues takes about 20-40 us against a fixed charge of 4,236 units for a 2,054-instruction program; it happens once per bucket per pass after a collection, a per-request constant the model omits.
- **CR-19.** Library: regexp.go:197-205 prefix/prefixBytes, inputString.index; exec.go:196-207 (NFA: Index when the run queue empties), backtrack.go:337-347 (Index per start position). Evidence: Code reading (confirmed): each Index call is linear in the distance to the next occurrence plus the prefix it verifies; the backtracker's repeated verifications are bounded by n x prefix <= maxBacktrackVector.
- **CR-20.** Library: regexp.go:225 matchPool (per bucket), backtrack.go:47 bitStatePool (job stack capacity up to 2^18 jobs kept), exec.go machine threads pooled with it. Evidence: Code reading: pools keep one object per P plus a victim cache until two collections, so up to peak concurrency x (about 2 MB machine with threads + 12.6 MB job stack) stays live after Release. Verifier probe TestVCPoolsTogether2: two NFA buckets (2,055 and 16,005 instructions) in one request peaked at 663,552 B, below the larger charge; the within-request sum was not reproduced (the decision pass stops at the first failing member).
- **CR-21.** Library: regexp/syntax Parse/Simplify/Compile called by regexcost.go:289-290. Evidence: Mutant m13 (regexcost.go:451 scan charge len(q) removed) survives TestQueueBoundBudget and TestClassRepsBudget: the test's 'sparse' case (built for this charge) runs 0.44 s against 0.11 s but asserts only an upper bound on the steps spent. Mutants m42 (classReps check) and m43 (heldAlternations budget) are killed.
- **CR-22.** Library: Go regexp MatchString (backtracker/NFA) from work.go:500-506 and states.go memberEntries. Evidence: Mutant m07 (work.go:494 budget check off) is killed by TestCompileMatchBudget (muts1.out).
- **CR-23.** Library: backtrack.go:129-135 jobs (<= 2^18 jobs), regexp.go:231-257 queues, exec.go:340 recursion. Evidence: Code reading: one match at a time, up to about 12.6 MB of job stack (2^18 jobs x 48 B with growth) or 0.8-2 MB of NFA state plus up to 2 MiB of stack (CR-15, CR-16).
- **CR-24.** Library: draft.go:127-135 meta-schemas compiled with AssertFormat; objcompiler.go:186-200 format regex -> RegexpEngine.validate (compiler.go:321-328); roots.go:238-246; meta/validation pattern and meta/applicator patternProperties propertyNames. Evidence: Mutant m06 (no cache store, schema.go:397) is killed by TestRegexpCache and TestReferenceLadder (muts1.out). Every compile path, $recursiveRef targets included, goes through the cache.
- **CR-25.** Library: validator.go:254-260 (patternProperties per member), :457 (pattern per string), :287-300 (propertyNames names). Evidence: Mutant m34 (value patterns not charged, working.go:1120) is killed by TestMatcherReach, TestHandlePatternFit and TestHandleBacktrackCharge (muts2.out).
- **CR-26.** Library: parse.go:1655-1757 aliases/unicodeTable; unicode/tables.go Version 15.0.0 (go1.26.8) vs 17.0.0 (go1.27.1). Evidence: Verified unicode.Version 15.0.0 in go1.26.8 and 17.0.0 in go1.27.1. Auditor probe: \p{Garay} refused on go1.26.8 and accepted on go1.27.1; \pL 659 vs 684 ranges (the measure differs); U+10D50 matches ^\pL+$ only on go1.27.1. Floor and release builds accept and match different schemas and bodies.
- **CR-27.** Library: perl_groups.go via parse.go:1585-1597 (\s = [\t\n\f\r ]), compile.go:84-85 anyRuneNotNL (. excludes only \n). Evidence: Code reading: ECMA \s also matches U+00A0, U+FEFF, U+2028/2029, U+3000 and \v; ECMA . excludes \r, U+2028, U+2029. So ^\S+$ accepts "a\<U+00A0>b" and a.b accepts "a\rb", unlike ECMA validators. The deviation follows the contract but is undocumented.
- **CR-28.** Library: regexp.go:166-212 compile keeps expr and subexpNames; patternsize.go:108 regexp.Compile(noOnePass + expr) allocates a new string; the library's Schema keeps the compiledPattern with the decoded text. Evidence: Verifier probe TestVCExprKept (zz_vc_text_test.go): "(?i)"x60000+"a" (240,001 bytes) measures 28 instructions (1,120 B) and the compiled Schema keeps 501,272 B. Bounded per document by about 2 x 256 KiB; doc.go's kept figure (16 MB) omits it.
- **CX-01.** Library: objcompiler.go:300-325 enqueueRef for $ref/$dynamicRef; roots.go:192-212 ensureSubschema meta-checks targets outside the keyword tree. Evidence: Mutant m24 (follow walks targets inert, walk.go:494) is killed by TestCompileSchemaFindings (muts1.out). Probe control: {"$ref":"#/x","x":{"pattern":30x[a-z]{1000}}} refused in 0.18 ms.
- **CX-02.** Library: objcompiler.go:56-59 compileDraft2019 runs for every draft >= 2019; :265-272 enqueueRef("$recursiveRef"). Evidence: Reconfirmed on the working tree (TestVCRecursiveRef, zz_vc_combo_test.go): {"$recursiveRef":"#/x","x":{"pattern":30x[a-z]{1000}}} compiles (nil) in 21 ms, refused under $ref; CR-01's POSIX pattern under it accepted in 6.0 s. Open round-9 finding 1 (runs/w3-s2-wp51-r9.json): panics, number bounds, MaxSubschemas, additionalItems check bypassed.
- **CX-03.** Library: objcompiler.go:269 enqueueRef resolves against the loader (enqueueRefVal :307-325, roots.resolveFragment) and the bundled meta-schemas. Evidence: Round-9 finding 1 item 5: {"$recursiveRef":"https://example.com/x"} and file:/// reach the loader (spy records the URL) and the finding lands at the document root. The refusing loader still blocks every fetch, and a bundled meta-schema target is refused by inspect after compiling, so no file or network access occurs: the defect is the loader-call property (T15, FuzzCompileSchema) and the diagnostic pointer, hence major rather than blocker.
- **CX-04.** Library: validator.go:769-779 resolveRecursiveAnchor (scp.sch.resource.RecursiveAnchor), :793-799 validateRefs; objcompiler.go:272 boolean("$recursiveAnchor"); the 2020-12 root meta-schema types $recursiveAnchor as an anchorString. Evidence: Auditor probe: {"$recursiveAnchor":true} is refused ('got boolean, want string'), but under a $recursiveRef target (CX-02) it compiles unchecked. Resolution stays static only because every resource root is meta-checked; nothing pins it. A dynamic-only in-place cycle through it would reach the library's RefCycle at run time.
- **CX-05.** Library: validator.go:781-791 resolveDynamicAnchor (map lookup hashing the name at every scope step), :803-808. Evidence: Open round-9 finding 3 (not re-measured): a 120 KB anchor and a 236 KB body take 33.1 s of decision pass charged 8.6% of the work limit.
- **CX-06.** Library: validator.go:286-300 validates each name with sch.validate (validator.go:19-33), whose scope holds only the propertyNames schema, so resolveDynamicAnchor (:781) never sees the outer resources. Evidence: Reconfirmed by verifier probe TestVCDynamicPropertyNames (zz_vc_combo_test.go): outer resource with $dynamicAnchor n and maxLength 1, $ref to resource mid with its own $dynamicAnchor n, propertyNames {"$dynamicRef":"#n"} and properties.p {"$dynamicRef":"#n"}: Validate({"o":{"ab":1}}) = ok, while {"o":{"p":"ab"}} fails at /maxLength. Draft 2020-12 resolves to the outermost anchor in both, so an invalid body is accepted.
- **CX-07.** Library: validator.go:554-635 condValidate (allOf stops in bool mode, anyOf evaluates all under uneval, oneOf bool after a match, not/if bool mode), :231-241 dependencies, :307-312 dependentSchemas. Evidence: Mutant m27 (fan-out check off, inspect.go:325) is killed by TestFanOutBound; m28 (nesting limit) by TestMinNesting and TestEvaluationBoundBudget (muts2.out).
- **CX-08.** Library: validator.go:818-866 error/addErr/addErrors (empty errors in bool mode), :659-683 validateSelf. Evidence: Mutant m33 (decision-pass error charge 0, working.go:1086) is killed by TestHandleBoundsValidationMemory and TestWorkingSetCharges (muts2.out).
- **CX-09.** Library: validator.go:79-86 checkCycle, :909-919 kwLoc (string building over the scope, reached only on a RefCycle), kind.RefCycle. Evidence: Mutant m30 (cycle findings dropped, work.go:604) is killed by TestInPlaceCycles (muts2.out). A dynamic-only cycle needs $recursiveAnchor true (CX-04).
- **CX-10.** Library: validator.go:684-702 validateVal per member/item. Evidence: Mutant m28 (work.go:1036 limit check off) is killed by TestMinNesting and TestEvaluationBoundBudget (muts2.out).
- **CX-11.** Library: validator.go validate -> condValidate/objValidate/validateRefs -> validateSelf -> validate per in-place evaluation. Evidence: Mutant m08 (own = 0, working.go:1089) is killed by TestStackWithinCharge (its child process overflows the stack set to the charge). Auditor probe: 691 to 933 B per in-place evaluation, within 8 KiB.
- **CX-12.** Library: same recursion as CX-11; runtime maxstacksize 1e9 < 1 GiB, so a goroutine cannot double past 512 MiB. Evidence: Auditor probe: at most 933 B per evaluation, so 4,096 x 64 needs about 233 MiB, under the 512 MiB step with a 2x margin; CR-16's add recursion adds at most a few MiB.
- **CX-13.** Library: validator.go:659-683 validateSelf (unevalFrom copy per in-place evaluation, merge), :920-956; measured through working_test.go:200-223 peakHeap (HeapInuse sampled every 100 us at GOGC 10) within gcSlack 1 MiB. Evidence: Open round-9 finding 4 (Go 1.26 floor under load). Verifier stress run on go1.27.1: three concurrent processes of the peakHeap tests at -test.count=4 (load 9.3): TestHandleUnevaluatedBranches failed in 2 of 3 (peak 6,561,792 and 7,331,840/7,602,176 B against 5,029,320 reserved + 1 MiB slack); the others passed. Whether sampled garbage or a real under-charge is not decided by the sampling.
- **CX-14.** Library: validator.go:659-683 (callerNeeds = uneval non-empty), :920-943 unevalFrom, :945-956 merge, :583-597 anyOf evaluates every branch under uneval. Evidence: Mutant m18 (uneval member work 0, working.go:1287) is killed by TestWorkCharges; m31 (markUneval does not propagate) by TestHandleBoundsUnevaluatedMemory (muts1/2.out).
- **CX-15.** Library: validator.go:243-273 member loop (properties lookup, delete), unevalFrom, duplicates/writeHash, equals. Evidence: Mutant m14 (lookup hashes 0, working.go:1316) is killed by TestWorkCharges and TestHandleWorkBound (muts1.out).
- **CX-16.** Library: validator.go:254-260 every regex per member, :286-300 propertyNames validates the name as a string. Evidence: Mutants m15 (name scan work removed, working.go:1082) and m35 (parent match dropped, working.go:1042) are killed by TestStatesMemberPatterns and TestWorkCharges (muts1/2.out). The memory side is CR-15/CR-16.
- **CX-17.** Library: compiler.go:297-303 queue.get (IndexFunc comparing urlPtr; equal-length pointers compare their whole common prefix), schema.go newSchema Location = up.String(). Evidence: Reconfirmed by verifier probe TestVCLongNameQueue (zz_vc_longname_test.go): one name of 4,000 bytes above 4,000 equal-length positions (44 KB schema) compiles in 2.57 s, 8,000 bytes in 4.46 s, against 0.20 s with a 1-byte name. Open round-9 finding 2 (20,000 bytes, 25 s, 162 MB kept). A long $id is not a member (auditor TestCrossLongID).
- **CX-18.** Library: validator.go:818-832 error keeps SchemaURL; WP-51 reads it per leaf. Evidence: Mutant m20 (walk charge removed, working.go:1074) is killed by TestHandleLocationWalk (muts1.out).
- **CX-19.** Library: Go map inserts and string compares in states.go build/next/memberEntries. Evidence: Auditor probe: one schema declaring a 120,000-byte name reached by 1,993 distinct states compiles in 0.29 s, as with a 1-byte name (0.34 s): negligible but unpinned.
- **CX-20.** Library: validator.go:243-273 loop over every member per evaluation. Evidence: Mutant m16 (member work removed, working.go:1283) is killed by TestWorkCharges and TestHandleWorkBound (muts1.out).
- **CX-21.** Library: roots.go:192-212 ensureSubschema validates every unprocessed target; roots.go:238-246 validate; root.go clone copies bookkeeping. Evidence: Mutants m26 (repeat count) and m25b (copy bound) are killed by TestMetaChecksBound (muts1/2.out). Not bounded via $recursiveRef: its targets are not in w.targets, so metaChecks neither orders nor counts their checks (round-9 finding 1 item 4: 40,000 subschemas under a $recursiveRef target took 17.2 s).
- **CX-22.** Library: meta-schema uniqueItems on type/required/dependentRequired/dependencies lists (duplicates/writeHash, math/big). Evidence: Mutants m21 (nameLists removed) killed by TestNameListsRefusedByWalk and TestNameListsMatchLibrary; m22 (checkNumbers removed) by TestCompileSchemaFindings (muts1.out). Not bounded via $recursiveRef: round-9 finding 1 item 1, a 260-byte type list of 21 huge literals under a $recursiveRef target panics in CompileSchema, Check and Factory.Build.
- **CX-23.** Library: library collects identifiers below additionalItems; compiles $dynamicAnchor targets eagerly (compiler.go:223-235). Evidence: Open round-9 minor 1: {"items":{...},"additionalItems":{"$id":"#item",...}} refused though nothing compiles or applies it.
- **CX-24.** Library: validator.go:286-300 (boolResult not passed on). Evidence: Mutants m19 (key errors, working.go:1350) and m32 (wrappers, :1349) are killed by TestWorkingSetCharges (muts1/2.out).
- **CX-25.** Library: validator.go:388-418 (matched slice per evaluation while it loops; kept in MinContains/MaxContains errors). Evidence: Mutant m46 (kept term removed, the location pass) is killed by TestHandleBoundsMatchedItems; mutant m17 (per-loop list term removed, working.go:1241) survives it: no test holds an outer contains loop's list alive while an inner item's contains runs (nested arrays), the shape the path term charges.
- **CX-26.** Library: validator.go:243-312 range over maps. Evidence: Mutant m29 (last leaf wins, validate.go:140) is killed by TestValidateKeywordLocation (muts2.out).
- **CX-27.** Library: objcompiler.go:116-125, :167-172 compile each pattern where its position compiles. Evidence: Mutant m23 (pattern rule removed, walk.go:449) is killed by TestPatternProgramLimits and TestCompileSchemaFindings (muts1.out). Not bounded via $recursiveRef (CX-02: 30x[a-z]{1000} accepted in 21 ms; round-9: seven 400x[a-z]{1000} patterns compile in 1.9 s, keep 118 MB).
- **CX-28.** Library: objcompiler.go:186-200 (format only under assertFormat: hasVocab format-assertion for 2020-12), compileDraft7/2019 content only under assertContent (contentSchema not enqueued). Evidence: Code reading (confirmed): s.Format, ContentEncoding, ContentMediaType stay nil for user schemas, so no body string is ever compiled as a regex or decoded. WP-51 compiles contentSchema positions itself as starts (schema.go:307-317), which only adds to the charges. Finalize (completeness check): reclassified from not-applicable, severity none, to partial, major. The format part arises at library-only positions (CC-01 to CC-04: asserted formats, body strings compiled as regexps, and the bundled meta/format-assertion dropping a sibling type keyword). The content part holds on every route: the library asserts content only under AssertContent (objcompiler.go:239, :305), which WP-51 never sets.
- **CX-29.** Library: metaschemas/draft/2020-12/meta/core ($defs anchorString, uriString, uriReferenceString; $id pattern) and root schema ($recursiveAnchor, $recursiveRef); draft.go meta-schemas compiled with AssertFormat and Go regexp; roots.go:238-246 validate. Evidence: Verifier probe TestVCMetaStrings (zz_vc_text_test.go): 250,000-byte $anchor compiles in 9.3 ms, $dynamicAnchor 6.6 ms, $id 10.6 ms, $comment 2.5 ms, an invalid anchor refused in 5.0 ms; the meta-schema's patterns have under 10 instructions, so the cost is linear per byte checked. Re-checks are bounded by metaChecks except via $recursiveRef targets (CX-21).
- **CX-30.** Library: compiler.go:223-235 compileValue: for every anchor of the resource, slices.Contains(res.dynamicAnchors, anchor), quadratic in the resource's anchors with string compares; root.go:126-131 collectAnchors appends every $dynamicAnchor. Evidence: Verifier probe TestVCDynamicAnchors (zz_vc_anchor_test.go): 4,000 $defs with $dynamicAnchor names of 35 bytes (251,739-byte schema) compile in 223 ms, of 6 bytes 196 ms, against 108 ms for the same document with $anchor; about 0.1 s uncharged on top of the combined worst case (0.95-1.2 s against F6's 1 s).

### 3.6 Completeness check (CC)

6 rows: 0 bounded, 0 partial, 6 gap, 0 not applicable. Library files: every function of the non-test files and kind/kind.go, the keyword strings of objcompiler.go and draft.go, the Go regexp entry points.

| ID | Feature | Cost | Status | Sev. | WP-51 bound | Tests | Gap fix | Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Then the library's request-time format 'regex' check calls WP-51's compile-time regexpCache, a plain map | P | gap | blocker | none: walk.go recordOnce refuses $schema other than Draft only at walked positions; inspect.go:169 checks DraftVersion only, which this dialect passes (2020) | none | Class fix (GC-21): (1) the inspector refuses any compiled schema whose Format, ContentEncoding, ContentMediaType or ContentSchema is set, with RZ-CFG-005 at that keyword, because 07 makes them annotations on every route; (2) the regexp engine given to the library stops being usable once compileWalked returns (frozen: a later call fails and does not cache), and no compiled Schema keeps a reference to the cache; (3) GC-01 and GC-02 close the routes. Deterministic tests: TestCompileSchemaFindings rows for each route and each vocabulary source (root $vocabulary, bundled meta/format-assertion), each refused with no load; a -race test that runs Handle in parallel on a compiled filter and asserts the engine is never called after compile. | GC-21 |
| CC-02 | Request-time regex format: every distinct body string is compiled and kept in regexpCache for the life of the Schema | C3 | gap | blocker | none | none | Closed by CC-01's fix (frozen engine plus the inspector refusing asserted formats). Test: retained heap does not grow across requests once compile has finished (an engine-call counter, which is deterministic, rather than heap sampling, per GC-08). | GC-21 |
| CC-03 | Request-time format work outside the working-set and work models: body strings compiled as regexps, and format validators that split strings per separator | V2 | gap | major | none (the work model charges no format CPU or memory) | none | Closed by CC-01's fix (format never asserted). If format assertion is ever wanted, it needs its own charges and an ADR. Test: CC-01's rows. | GC-21 |
| CC-04 | Format asserted on bodies, against doc.go:22 and 07, on library-only routes; the vocabulary subset also drops the validation and applicator keywords there | W | gap | major | none; VLU-41's 'bounded' status holds only on walked routes | none (VLU-41's mutant assert_format covers only the compiler flag) | Closed by CC-01's fix for format; the dropped validation and applicator keywords close with GC-01 and GC-02 (the walk's $schema rule at every position the library compiles). Test: CC-01's rows assert RZ-CFG-005 at the format keyword. | GC-21 |

The DraftVersion backstop runs only after the library has compiled | P | gap | blocker | none on library-only routes (walk.go recordOnce applies only at walked positions) | none | GC-01 and GC-02's class fix (rules, including recordOnce, at every position the library compiles), plus refusing $schema other than Draft before c.Compile on every route. Tests: TestCompileSchemaFindings rows for older-draft enum lists on each route, refused with RZ-CFG-005 and no panic in CompileSchema, Check and Factory.Build. | GC-02 |
| CC-06 | Meta-check uniqueItems hash collisions among composite list entries at library-only positions (writeHash writes a tag byte per array or object and no lengths), so duplicates() compares colliding entries pairwise | C1 | gap | minor | walk.go:107-139 nameLists bounds it at walked positions only (VLU-53) | none on library-only routes | GC-01 and GC-02's class fix (nameLists at every library-compiled position). Test: TestNameListsRefused rows on each library-only route, asserting a refusal before the library compiles. | GC-02 |

#### Library paths and evidence (CC)

- **CC-01.** Library: roots.go:136-146 dialect from getMetaVocabs (loader.go:196-218, draft.go:181-223); objcompiler.go:436-445 assertFormat true when hasVocab('format-assertion'); objcompiler.go:188-192 Format{Validate: roots.regexpEngine.validate}, bound to WP-51's engine; validator.go:136-142 calls it on every string evaluation (regexpEngine is nil under Schema.Validate, so Format.Validate runs); WP-51 schema.go:383-403 regexpCache.compile writes the map with no lock. Evidence: Overlay probe in scratchpad/wp51audit/complete. CompileSchema accepts the shape through both the $recursiveRef route and the base-mismatch route, and through the bundled meta/format-assertion meta-schema with no root $vocabulary. Calling Handle from 8 goroutines killed the test process with 'fatal error: concurrent map writes' (or 'concurrent map read and map write') in 3 of 3 runs. That error cannot be recovered, so one config.schema can bring ruralzd down under load. Finalize re-run (probe4.test, TestCCBundledFormatAssertion, load 0.05): 'fatal error: concurrent map read and map write' again.
- **CC-02.** Library: validator.go:136-142 -> compiler.go:321-328 RegexpEngine.validate -> WP-51 schema.go:391-403 (rc[expr] = r). Evidence: Probe: after 2,000 distinct 1 KB body strings, about 100 MB stays on the heap after GC (about 50 KB per string). It grows without limit across requests, outside every reservation.
- **CC-03.** Library: format.go:42-708 (strings.Split in validateJSONPointer, validateUUID, validateIPV4, validateURITemplate, validateSemver; LocalizableError values, util.go:452-467); WP-51 patternsize.go:104-113 compilePattern on body text; WP-51 working.go:440 and :632 count Format only as an error unit. Evidence: Probe at load 0.1: one 180 KB body string compiled as a regexp took 125 ms and allocated 76 MB. The json-pointer format on a 1 MiB body allocated 16 MB more than the same body with no format.
- **CC-04.** Library: objcompiler.go:89-183 hasVocab gates; objcompiler.go:185-200; draft.go:232-240 dialect.hasVocab. Evidence: Probe: valid strings pass and invalid ones are refused (Respond) for the regex, email, hostname, date-time and uuid formats. With the bundled meta/format-assertion, a sibling type keyword is silently dropped.
- **CC-05.** Library: roots.go:100-110 getDraft and resource dialect; roots.go:237-245 validate with metaschemas/draft-0{4,6,7}/schema (properties/enum uniqueItems); util.go:396-400 writeHash; WP-51 inspect.go:169-171 runs after c.Compile. Evidence: Probe: CompileSchema panicked (nil pointer dereference) for draft-07 and draft-04 resources through both the $recursiveRef and base-mismatch routes; the same list under the 2020-12 dialect compiles. CMP-22 and VLU-51 name only the type, required, dependentRequired and dependencies lists.
- **CC-06.** Library: util.go:334-364 duplicates, util.go:366-405 writeHash; metaschemas 2020-12 stringArray/type uniqueItems (and enum under older drafts, CC-05). Evidence: Probe: compile time grows quadratically with the entry count. A list of about 217 KB took 224 ms on a $recursiveRef target against 16 ms on the walked twin, and 305 ms under an older-draft dialect. MaxSchemaBytes holds it to roughly 0.3 to 0.5 s, against the 1 s compile budget.

## 4. Gap classes

Every partial and gap row of section 3 belongs to exactly one class below, grouped by root cause. Each class has one fix for the whole class and the tests it needs: one per member and per small edge case, each failing without the fix. Some members also need another class's fix before they close; the "also needs" column says which.

### 4.2 Order and dependencies

- GC-01 and GC-02 come first: they change which positions the walk sees, and the tables of GC-16, the budget of GC-03, the index rule of GC-04 and the deterministic findings of GC-19 (REFS-14) are written over the routes they open. GC-01 and GC-02 share the backstop (no compiled position the walk did not walk live; recover into RZ-CFG-005).
- GC-21's parts (1) and (2), the inspector refusal of a compiled Format or content field and the regexp engine frozen after compile, depend on no other class and on their own close the process crash of CC-01 (one config.schema can end ruralzd under load). Do them first, with GC-01 and GC-02, which close its routes (part 3).
- GC-03 and GC-09 are one compile budget with byte terms (GC-03) and count terms (GC-09); design them together, with one contract request.
- GC-08 comes before any test that measures memory (GC-05's TestWorkingSetCoversPeak rows, GC-11, GC-12, GC-17's CX-25 row).
- GC-11's add() depth feeds GC-15's stack term.
- Contract requests and owner decisions needed: GC-03 (byte budget and its limit), GC-04 (canonical indexes only), GC-05 (refuse or clamp values above int64), GC-09 (count terms, if they refuse), GC-15 (absolute stack ceiling), and the alternatives named in GC-02, GC-07, GC-11, GC-19 and GC-20. GC-21 needs none: 07 req 75 already makes format and the content keywords annotations. Every refusal uses existing codes (RZ-CFG-005 at compile, 413 RZ-RT-003 at run time); no new code, field or Policy type is needed.

### GC-01. $recursiveRef and $recursiveAnchor are not first-class references in WP-51

Severity: blocker. Members: 35.

**Root cause.** The library runs its draft 2019-09 compile layer under draft 2020-12 (objcompiler.go:56-59) and compiles $recursiveRef with enqueueRef (objcompiler.go:269), so it is a live, static reference. The walk records reference sites only for $ref and $dynamicRef (walk.go:346). Every rule, count and check the walk applies to a reference target is therefore skipped for a $recursiveRef target: number bounds, name lists, the pattern measure, MaxSubschemas, the meta re-check order and count, the additionalItems check, checkRef (loader, meta-schema, missing target), and the diagnostic pointer. At run time the model knows $recursiveRef (inspect.go:52, work.go:355, work.go:446, working.go:439 and 595), but no test file mentions it, so those terms are unproven, and $recursiveAnchor's dynamic retarget is not modelled.

| CMP-22 | compile | P | partial | minor | Meta-check uniqueItems on number list entries panics (writeHash nil Num) | - |
| CMP-23 | compile | C1 | partial | minor | Meta-check format 'regex' compiles every pattern value and patternProperties name; the compile compiles them again | GC-02 |

**Fix for the class.** Round-9 fix (a), the only option without a restricted profile. (1) Define the library's reference keywords once ($ref, $dynamicRef, $recursiveRef) and use that list in walk.go (site list at :346, follow, checkRef, w.targets, checkOrder, uncheckedPositions), schema.go loadIssues, inspect.go:52 and work.go:557 isReference. Pin the list with a test against the library's enqueueRef call sites (objcompiler.go:90, 269, 319). A $recursiveRef target is then walked live, counted, ordered and counted by metaChecks, checked apart below additionalItems, and refused at /$recursiveRef when it is remote, a meta-schema or missing. (2) After compile, refuse with RZ-CFG-005 any compiled Schema with RecursiveAnchor true (a backstop: once every compiled resource root is meta-checked, a boolean $recursiveAnchor is already refused), instead of modelling the retarget. (3) Defense in depth for run-time number work: derive the schema side of number work from the compiled values (ratDigits of each bound, the uncapped exponent of each const and enum literal) rather than capping at MaxNumberExponent. (4) Document in doc.go, next to the dependencies exception, that the library enforces $recursiveRef statically under 2020-12; correct doc.go:19-21 and the fuzz_test.go:56-70 comment. The class-wide backstop (no compiled position the walk did not walk live; recover into RZ-CFG-005) is part of GC-02.

**Tests.**

A $recursiveRef twin of every reference test, each through CompileSchema, Check and Factory.Build, expecting RZ-CFG-005 at the target pointer, no panic and an empty spy loader:
- numbers: each parsedKeywords entry at 1e9999999, 1e1001 and a 129-byte literal behind $recursiveRef (shares GC-16's table; VLU-51), and 20 literals of 1e999999 refused by the walk before the library runs, with an allocation bound (VLU-52);
- name lists: type, required, dependentRequired and dependencies lists of 21 x 1e9999999 (CMP-21, CMP-22, CX-22);
- patterns: 30 x [a-z]{1000} as pattern and as a patternProperties name; seven 400 x [a-z]{1000} patterns (CMP-23, CMP-24, CMP-51, CX-27);
- counts: 12,000 properties and an allOf of 40,000 under the target (REFS-11, CX-21);
- meta re-checks: a $recursiveRef reference ladder and a copies case in TestMetaChecksBound, and a 64 KB $anchor ladder (CX-21, CX-29);
- additionalItems: {"$recursiveRef":"#/additionalItems","additionalItems":{"multipleOf":0}} and each additionalItemsCases payload behind $recursiveRef; Validate(5) never panics (CMP-26, CMP-34, REFS-13, VAL-49);
- loader and findings: https and file: values, the 2020-12 and 2019-09 meta-schema URLs, '#nope', '#/missing', '#/required', a target with a custom $schema, a target holding $ref 'y.json' (CMP-31, CMP-35, REFS-12, REFS-13, REFS-25, CX-03);
- $recursiveAnchor true at the root, under $defs, behind $recursiveRef, below additionalItems with and without $id, plus the post-compile backstop with an injected RecursiveAnchor (CX-04, VAL-48, REFS-15);
- run time: TestWorkCharges and TestWorkingSetCharges rows for {"$recursiveRef":"#/$defs/x"} passing and failing (VAL-47, REFS-32, REFS-33, REFS-34); TestInPlaceCycles rows for a self cycle, a two-schema cycle and a cycle through allOf (REFS-31); TestEvaluationBoundMatchesLibrary, TestFanOut and TestInspectorBackstop rows (REFS-25, REFS-34); a TestValidateKeywordLocation row; a TestWorkCharges row with const [1e99999] and minimum 1e99999 at a target, pinning the compiled-value charge (VLU-16);
- documentation: a validate_test row pinning static enforcement and its keyword location (CMP-37);
- the list-pinning test, and FuzzCompileSchema seeds for every shape above.

**Contract.** None beyond documenting the static enforcement (round-9 fix (a)).

### GC-02. The walk's model of which positions the library compiles, with which base and in which order, is not the library's

Severity: blocker. Members: 25.

**Root cause.** follow (walk.go:484-496) walks a JSON Pointer target once, in site order, with the resource and URL the reference named (walk.go:494). The library bases a target, and every reference inside it, on the longest pointer prefix that is a resource it has collected by then (roots.go:216-235, root.go:20-32), collects the $ids of targets checked apart in pointer order, and compiles exactly what references reach. So the library compiles positions the walk never walked live (base mismatch, an $id recorded by a later follow, an ancestor target's $id), resolves their references elsewhere, and reaches the loader. In the other direction the walk makes positions live that the library never compiles: identifies() (walk.go:364-377) makes a whole additionalItems subtree live for any identifier, and inert follows record anchors and $ids (walk.go:327-339, 387-396) that live references then rely on. The completeness check found two more consequences of rules missing at library-only positions: a $schema naming draft-04, -06 or -07 there makes the library meta-check the resource with that draft's meta-schema, whose uniqueItems on enum reaches the writeHash nil-Rat panic before the inspector's DraftVersion backstop runs (inspect.go:169-171, after c.Compile) (CC-05); and the meta-check's uniqueItems over composite list entries, which nameLists bounds only at walked positions, hashes them with colliding keys and compares them pairwise (CC-06).

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CMP-27 | compile | W | partial | minor | identifies() makes a whole additionalItems subtree live for any $id, $anchor or $dynamicAnchor in it | - |
| CMP-39 | compile | S | partial | minor | $ref / $dynamicRef to a remote URL | GC-01 |
| CMP-42 | compile | W | partial | minor | $ref resolution errors: missing pointer, non-schema target, bad escape, missing anchor, anchor of another resource | - |
| CMP-45 | compile | D | partial | minor | Duplicate $id and duplicate $anchor/$dynamicAnchor | - |
| CMP-52 | compile | W | partial | minor | pattern: RE2 dialect versus ECMA-262 | - |
| CMP-68 | compile | S | gap | major | NEW. Base mismatch: a JSON Pointer reference to a position under an embedded resource but outside its keyword tree is walked under the referencing resource's base, while the library uses the enclosing resource's base, so the library resolves that position's relative references elsewhere and reaches the loader | - |
| CMP-69 | compile | P | gap | blocker | NEW. Base mismatch: the library compiles a position the walk walked inert (below additionalItems, no identifier), so no rule and no meta-schema check covers it: panic at validation | - |
| CMP-70 | compile | C1 | gap | blocker | NEW. Base mismatch: rule bypass at the escaped position (number bounds, pattern measure, name lists, position count) | - |
| CMP-71 | compile | W | gap | minor | NEW. Base mismatch: wrong results (valid schemas refused, library errors at the root, anchors and $id recorded in the wrong resource) | - |
| REFS-01 | refs | P | gap | blocker | Base URI of a JSON Pointer reference target outside the keyword tree that lies below an $id resource of the keyword tree (the library takes the longest pointer-prefix resource; the walk takes the resource the reference's URL named) | - |
| REFS-02 | refs | C1 | gap | blocker | Base-mismatch route: walk rules and counts bypassed (number bounds, pattern bounds, name lists, MaxSubschemas, check order, maxCheckCopies) | - |

| REFS-08 | refs | W | gap | blocker | Late-$id route: unchecked additionalItems and $schema dialect | - |
| REFS-09 | refs | S | gap | minor | $id the walk records that the library has not collected when the reference compiles (check order of targets; $id recorded by an inert follow) | - |
| REFS-16 | refs | S | partial | major | $schema at every collected position (getDraft runs before the $id check; non-draft URLs are loaded) | GC-01 |

| CX-23 | cross | W | gap | minor | additionalItems x identifiers ($id, $anchor, $dynamicAnchor) making an unreferenced subtree live | - |
| CC-05 | completeness | P | gap | blocker | Older-draft dialect (draft-04, -06 or -07 $schema with id/$id) at a library-only position: the library meta-checks it with that draft's meta-schema, which applies uniqueItems to enum, so the existing writeHash nil-Rat panic class reaches enum lists. The DraftVersion backstop runs only after the library has compiled | GC-01 |
| CC-06 | completeness | C1 | gap | minor | Meta-check uniqueItems hash collisions among composite list entries at library-only positions (writeHash writes a tag byte per array or object and no lengths), so duplicates() compares colliding entries pairwise | GC-01 |

**Fix for the class.** Make the walk compute exactly the set of positions the library compiles, each with the library's base. (1) Keep a pointer-to-resource map of every resource the library has collected when it compiles each target: keyword-tree resources, then the targets checked apart in pointer order and their keyword trees. Base every target, and every reference inside it, on the longest pointer prefix in that map. (2) Follow to a fixpoint (repeat until no new live position, $id or anchor is recorded), independent of site order. (3) Record $ids and anchors only on live walks. Make an additionalItems subtree live only through an identifier some reference can reach, plus $dynamicAnchor (the only identifier the library compiles eagerly, compiler.go:223-236), and follow plain-name anchor fragments in follow(). (4) With (1) to (3), the walk's rules run at every position the library compiles, so recordOnce refuses every $schema other than Draft and nameLists every non-string list entry before c.Compile on every route, older-draft dialects included (CC-05, CC-06). Backstops: after compile, refuse with RZ-CFG-005 any compiled Schema whose Location is not a walked-live position; recover a library panic during compile into RZ-CFG-005 in CompileSchema, Check and Factory.Build (a backstop only, since the CPU is spent before the panic). (5) FuzzCompileSchema properties: compiled positions are a subset of walked-live positions, the loader is never asked, and two compiles give identical issues; a differential test against a walk-free library compile for the shapes the walk accepts (as TestNameListsMatchLibrary does).

**Tests.**

Each through CompileSchema, Check and Factory.Build with a spy loader, expecting RZ-CFG-005 at the reference or position and no panic:
- base mismatch: the type bomb at /$defs/x/zzz (REFS-01); minimum 1e99999, 30 x [a-z]{1000} and 15,000 properties at the library-only target (REFS-02, CMP-70); a relative $ref 'f.json' and a remote $schema there (REFS-03, REFS-16, CMP-39, CMP-68); additionalItems {multipleOf:0} reached as 'c.json#/additionalItems', and a meta/core $schema (REFS-04, CMP-69); anchors in the wrong resource in both spellings, a $dynamicRef anchor, and the relative reference the walk over-refuses (REFS-05, CMP-42, CMP-71); a duplicate anchor the walk misses and one it invents (CMP-45); an invalid regex at a mismatched target below additionalItems (CMP-52);
- late $id: both REFS-06 shapes; pattern, number and MaxSubschemas shapes behind a late $id (REFS-07); additionalItems {multipleOf:0} and the meta/core dialect behind a late $id (REFS-08); 'Q1 check order' and 'Q2 inert id' (REFS-09);
- ancestor target $id, in both site orders (REFS-47);
- $schema naming policy.json and check.json at a library-only position, and a root $vocabulary with urn:unknown (REFS-49);
- inert anchor: {"additionalItems":{"$ref":"#/y"},"y":{"$anchor":"a"},"$ref":"#a"} refused at /$ref with the walk's message (REFS-39);
- over-refusal: unreferenced additionalItems with $id '#item', $anchor, $id 'x.json' and $dynamicAnchor next to an invalid sibling, accepted when unreferenced and refused when a reference reaches them; nested identifiers under items, allOf and not/not (round-9 minor 6, E1) (CMP-27, CX-23);
- older drafts at library-only positions: a draft-04 resource (id plus the draft-04 $schema) and a draft-07 one ($id plus the draft-07 $schema), each with enum [0, 1, ..., 19, 1e9999999], behind $recursiveRef and behind a base mismatch, refused at their $schema with RZ-CFG-005 and no panic (CC-05);
- the meta-check hash collisions: a required list of 8,000 colliding nested arrays (about 217 KB) behind $recursiveRef and behind a base mismatch, and the same list as an enum under a draft-07 $schema, each refused with the walk's own finding (nameLists, or the $schema rule) before the library compiles; the test asserts the finding and its pointer, never a time (CC-06);
- the backstop with an injected compiled position the walk did not walk, the recover path, and the fuzz properties.

**Contract.** None for the fix above. The alternative (refuse a pointer reference into an embedded resource outside its keyword tree) needs a contract request against 07 req 75.

### GC-03. String bytes times uses at compile: locations, pointers and URLs

Severity: blocker. Members: 23.

**Root cause.** MaxSchemaBytes bounds the document and MaxSubschemas the number of schema positions. Nothing bounds the product of a string's length and the number of times the library or WP-51 builds, hashes, compares or keeps it. The strings are pointers and URLs: one per schema position (queue compare and kept Location), per value schema or not (the meta-check's instance pointer, the library's collect, WP-51's walk and refusal path), per reference (base URI, target pointer) and per resource (nested $ids under a long base). One long member name or $id multiplies by thousands of uses inside a small document.

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CMP-02 | compile | C1 | gap | blocker | Compile work queue x location bytes: long member names in properties, $defs, definitions, dependentSchemas, dependencies and patternProperties maps (queue.get compares equal-length locations in full) | - |
| CMP-03 | compile | C2 | gap | blocker | Compile work queue x location bytes (long member names): compile peak memory | - |
| CMP-04 | compile | C3 | gap | major | Location strings kept by every compiled Schema (and c.schemas keys) for long member names | - |
| CMP-05 | compile | C1 | gap | blocker | The library's meta-check builds the instance pointer of every value it applies the meta root ('#meta') to (metaResource via handleMeta); the compile re-derives pointers per position (root.resource prefix walk, urlPtr.lookup, location strings) | - |
| CMP-09 | compile | C1 | partial | minor | Bookkeeping copy per reference target checked apart (ensureSubschema clones resources, anchors and the processed set) | - |
| CMP-11 | compile | C1 | gap | blocker | Base-URI resolution per reference under a long $id (each $ref, $dynamicRef or $recursiveRef reparses the base URL) | - |
| CMP-12 | compile | C2 | gap | blocker | Base-URI resolution per reference under a long $id: compile peak memory | - |
| CMP-64 | compile | C1 | gap | blocker | NEW. References to a long target pointer through an $id or resource URL: each short reference ('urn:r', 'urn:r#/x') resolves to a pointer under a long member name, which the library and the walk copy, compare and sort per reference | - |
| CMP-65 | compile | C2 | gap | blocker | NEW. References to a long target pointer: compile peak memory | - |
| CMP-66 | compile | C1 | gap | blocker | NEW. Long base URI x nested resources: every nested $id is joined against the long base, and the library scans all resources comparing whole ids (equal-length ids that differ only at the end) | - |
| CMP-67 | compile | C2 | gap | major | NEW. Long base URI x nested resources: compile peak memory | - |
| CMP-72 | compile | C1 | gap | blocker | NEW. WP-51 walk builds a pointer string for every element and member it visits, schema or not, before knowing there is a finding: checkNumbers over const and enum values, eachSubschema over every array item and map member of the subschema keywords | - |
| CMP-73 | compile | C2 | gap | blocker | NEW. WP-51 walk per-element pointer strings: compile peak memory | - |
| CMP-74 | compile | C1 | gap | blocker | NEW. Refusal path: metaIssues builds, hashes and sorts a full pointer for every leaf of the library's error tree, and stringEntries builds one for every failing list entry before the MaxIssues cap | - |
| CMP-75 | compile | C2 | gap | blocker | NEW. Refusal path pointer building: peak memory | - |
| CMP-76 | compile | C1 | gap | blocker | NEW. The library's collectResources builds a pointer for every item and member of every subschema keyword (schema or not) and keys subschemasProcessed by it | - |
| REFS-18 | refs | C1 | gap | blocker | Location bytes of compiled positions: long member names (round-9 finding 2, open) | - |
| REFS-20 | refs | C3 | gap | major | Memory the compiled Schema keeps for long locations (names and index spellings) | - |
| REFS-22 | refs | C1 | gap | major | Non-schema values at subschema positions (meta-schema refuses them): collected by the library, not counted by the walk | - |
| VLU-30 | values | C2 | gap | blocker | Schema member-name length times the positions below it: every location string the library and WP-51 build (escape doubles ~ and /, PathEscape triples non-ASCII), compared in the work queue and rebuilt by the meta-check | - |
| VLU-54 | values | C1 | gap | blocker | URL resolution at every reference: the library re-parses and re-serializes the resource's base URL ($id) for each $ref, $dynamicRef and $recursiveRef it compiles, and WP-51's walk does it twice more per reference site | - |
| VLU-55 | values | C3 | gap | blocker | Memory a compiled Schema keeps for long member names: every compiled schema's Location (baseURL#percent-encoded pointer), the urlPtr keys of the compiler's schema map and WP-51's per-node pointers, each holding the whole name | - |
| CX-17 | cross | C1 | gap | blocker | Long member names in the schema (properties, $defs, definitions, dependentSchemas, dependencies, patternProperties keys incl. long regexes) x the library's locations and quadratic queue search | - |

**Fix for the class.** One walk-time compile budget in bytes, refused with RZ-CFG-005 at the member, value, reference or $id that crosses it, before anything builds the string. It charges, computed incrementally from the parent's length and never by building strings: (a) every value of every subschema keyword, schema or not, at the length of its pointer as the library spells it (escape, then PathEscape per token); (b) every schema position, its location bytes times the equal-length queued entries the library compares it with (or a cap on location bytes per position); (c) every reference, base URI plus value plus resolved target pointer; (d) every resource, its resolved $id length times (resources + positions). The limit is an owner decision; ordinary schemas of 4,096 positions use about 0.25 to 0.5 MB. In the same fix: the walk refuses a non-schema value at a subschema position with the meta-schema's message before the library collects it (REFS-22); WP-51 builds pointer strings only for reported findings (checkNumbers, eachSubschema, stringEntries; metaIssues groups leaves by token slices and builds at most MaxIssues pointers), keeps parent indexes instead of full pointers in the walker, inspector and analysis, parses each base URL once, deduplicates w.targets, and compares pointers without rescanning decimal tokens.

**Tests.**

One per member and keyword, each through CompileSchema, Check and Factory.Build, asserting the refusal and an allocation bound (compileAlloc), never time:
- long ASCII, '/', '~' and non-ASCII names over many positions under properties, $defs, definitions, dependentSchemas, dependencies and patternProperties, and ten nested 1,000-byte names (CMP-02, CMP-03, REFS-18, VLU-30, CX-17);
- kept memory (heap after GC with the Schema live) at the largest accepted budget (CMP-04, REFS-20, VLU-55);
- the 'copies' shape of TestMetaChecksBound with its properties under a long name (CMP-09);
- non-schema items of allOf, anyOf, oneOf and prefixItems and members of properties, $defs, dependentSchemas and patternProperties, under a long ancestor name and at 100,000 short entries (CMP-05, CMP-76, REFS-22);
- enum and const arrays and objects under a long name, with a walk allocation bound that fails today (CMP-72, CMP-73);
- a type list of 30,000 'x' and required of 30,000 numbers under a long name, findings unchanged (CMP-74, CMP-75);
- a 60 KB, 100 KB and 200 KB $id with thousands of $ref, anchor, $dynamicRef and $recursiveRef references, and nested relative $ids (CMP-11, CMP-12, VLU-54);
- 'urn:r', 'urn:r#/x' and '#/...' references to a target under a 150,000-byte letter or decimal name (CMP-64, CMP-65);
- N = 250, 500 and 1,000 nested $ids under a 50 KB and 100 KB base, one and two levels deep (CMP-66, CMP-67);
- the largest accepted shape of each member in analysisCases; doc.go's compile time, allocation and kept-memory figures restated.

**Contract.** A new compile rule: contract request against 07 req 75 and R-77, with the limit as an owner decision (round-9 finding 2 already asks for it).

### GC-04. Non-canonical JSON Pointer array indexes in reference fragments

Severity: blocker. Members: 8.

**Root cause.** The library's urlPtr.lookup (util.go:234) and WP-51's lookup (pointer.go:113) both use strconv.Atoi, so '00', '+0' and '-0' all name element 0. Each spelling is a distinct urlPtr that the library meta-checks and compiles again with its whole subtree (roots.go:193-214, compiler.go:153-164). metaChecks counts repeats by pointer string (metacheck.go:91-118), so aliases escape the repeat bounds, and r.resource matches pointer prefixes as strings, so an alias below a resource resolves against a different base.

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CMP-06 | compile | C1 | gap | blocker | JSON Pointer array-index aliases in references ('#/x/0', '#/x/00', '#/x/+0', '#/x/-0', ...): every spelling is a new position the library meta-checks and compiles again | - |
| CMP-07 | compile | C2 | gap | blocker | JSON Pointer array-index aliases: compile peak memory | - |
| CMP-08 | compile | W | gap | minor | JSON Pointer array-index aliases: wrong result (a non-RFC 6901 pointer resolves, and the detail names the alias) | - |
| CMP-10 | compile | C1 | partial | minor | Meta-check repeated under reference ladders and member maps read as schemas | GC-01 |
| CMP-18 | compile | C1 | partial | minor | Meta-schema check per position (the draft 2020-12 meta-schema, precompiled at init with AssertFormat) | GC-02, GC-03 |
| CMP-25 | compile | C1 | partial | minor | Meta-check formats uri/uri-reference ($id, $ref, $dynamicRef, $recursiveRef, $schema, $vocabulary names) and the anchorString and $id patterns | - |
| REFS-19 | refs | C1 | gap | blocker | JSON Pointer array index spellings in reference fragments (leading zeros, '+', '-0'): each spelling a new position compiled again under a long prefix | - |
| REFS-48 | refs | W | gap | minor | Non-canonical array index below a resource: the library and the walk both resolve inner references against the enclosing pointer-prefix resource that the spelling misses | - |

**Fix for the class.** resolveRef and lookup refuse an array-index token that is not RFC 6901 canonical ('0' or [1-9][0-9]\*) with 'does not resolve' at the reference, for $ref, $dynamicRef and $recursiveRef, before the library runs. Key metaCount.checked and walker.seen by the decoded value's identity rather than the pointer string.

**Tests.**

- '00', '+0', '-0', '01' and '+00' refused at /$ref, /$dynamicRef and /$recursiveRef with no library call, and TestLookup rows (CMP-06, CMP-08, REFS-19);
- the n=450 alias document over a 75,000-item enum and the 222 KB N=200,000 document refused in milliseconds through CompileSchema, Check and Factory.Build, with a compileAlloc bound (CMP-07, REFS-19);
- the alias shape in TestMetaChecksBound (CMP-10, CMP-18);
- uri-format strings under an aliased subtree (CMP-25);
- the resource-below-index case of REFS-48 refused at the reference;
- a FuzzCompileSchema seed.

**Contract.** Refusing spellings the library accepts: contract request against 07 req 75 (canonical RFC 6901 indexes only).

### GC-05. Integer keyword values above int64

Severity: blocker. Members: 3.

**Root cause.** intVal (objcompiler.go:499-505) keeps the low 64 bits of minLength, maxLength, minItems, maxItems, minProperties, maxProperties, minContains and maxContains; WP-51's number bounds admit 128 digits, and the meta-schema accepts any non-negative integer. Validity is then wrong, and a value that wraps to a negative int aliases the -1 'absent' sentinel of lengthUnits (working.go:523-533), so the working set is not charged.

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CMP-56 | compile | W | gap | minor | Integer keywords above int64 wrap (intVal) | - |
| VLU-17 | values | W | gap | major | Integer keyword values above int64 (maxLength, minLength, maxItems, minItems, maxProperties, minProperties, maxContains, minContains): intVal wraps them, so validity is wrong | - |
| VLU-50 | values | V2 | gap | blocker | String length units when maxLength wraps to a negative int: the scan's -1 'absent' sentinel collides with the wrapped value, so a maxLength that fails every string is charged as never failing | - |

**Fix for the class.** At the walk, for every value of the eight integer keywords above math.MaxInt64: either refuse with RZ-CFG-005 (owner decision, contract row beside R-77's number rules) or compile a clamped copy of the document (MaxInt64 is equivalent for every body; Raw and Sum unchanged). Independently, make lengthUnits, and every other model field read from the compiled schema, keep presence apart from the value, so no compiled value can alias a sentinel.

**Tests.**

- one row per keyword at 2^63, 2^64, 2^64+1, 1e19, 1e30 and 1e400, checking validity (CMP-56, VLU-17);
- TestWorkingSetCharges and TestWorkingSetCoversPeak rows for maxLength 2^63, 2^64-1 and 1e19 (decision and location trees within the charge), a lengthUnits unit test with a negative compiled max, and a Handle row at the default limit expecting 413 for the 50-branch allOf body (VLU-50).

**Contract.** Refusal needs a contract row beside R-77's number rules (round-9 minor 3 already proposes it); clamping needs a doc.go note.

### GC-06. Regex parser work that the lexical pass does not count

Severity: blocker. Members: 5.

**Root cause.** Each pattern is parsed four times (patternsize.go:163, :105, :108; regexcost.go:289). The lexical scan (patternsize.go:269-298) charges Unicode escapes and fold work, but not every superlinear step of regexp/syntax: parseNamedClass searches to the end of the expression for ':]' at every '[:' (CR-01); under (?i) a Unicode class appends its table and fold table before cleanClass merges them, and the scan charges the cleaned result (CR-02); alternation factoring copies rune slices per level (CR-05). The ASCII fold charge has no test (CR-04), and the transient memory of one pattern's compile is never measured (CR-09).

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CR-01 | cross | C1 | gap | blocker | Regex parse: POSIX named-class lookahead inside a bracket class ('[:' with no later ':]'), parsed 4 times per pattern | - |
| CR-02 | cross | C1 | partial | major | Regex parse: Unicode class escapes (\p, \P, \p{^..}) table expansion and merge, plain and under (?i) | - |
| CR-04 | cross | C1 | partial | minor | Regex parse: Perl (\d\s\w) and POSIX ([:alpha:]) classes under (?i), inside and outside brackets | - |
| CR-05 | cross | C1 | partial | minor | Regex parse: alternation factoring (common literal prefixes, common leading classes, class merging), recursive per level | - |
| CR-09 | cross | C2 | partial | minor | Regex compile transient memory (parser nodes and class buffers, Simplify expansion, Inst slice growth) | - |

**Fix for the class.** Make the lexical pass an upper bound of the parser's work per construct, derived from regexp/syntax and checked against it, times the four parses, refused before any parse past MaxPatternInstructions or MaxDocumentInstructions: charge each '[:' inside a class with no later ':]' at the bytes Index scans (len(expr)-i); (?i) Unicode escapes at the ranges the parser appends (tab plus fold table, strided ranges per rune); ASCII Perl and POSIX groups at the runes appendFoldedClass folds; alternation factoring at its rune copies (or a lexical bound on nested common prefixes); and the compile's transient at the Regexp nodes Simplify builds. Re-measure the per-instruction rates against the four parses.

**Tests.**

- a parse-counter test (TestPatternParsesBounded style): '[:' runs of 10K, 40K and 130K refused before syntax.Parse, the shape without the closing ']', many '[[:a]' classes, a ':]' present, and the same as a patternProperties name (CR-01);
- (?i)\p{Ll}, (?i)\p{Lu}, (?i)\p{Lt} and (?i)\P{L}, asserting counted escapes equal appended ranges; the 8 x (?i)[\p{Ll}x224] document in BenchmarkCompileAnalysisWorstCase and the F6 figures (CR-02);
- literal expected counts for \d, \s, \w, their negations and each POSIX name under (?i), and refusal of (?i)[[:alpha:]xN] and (?i)\dxN before parsing (CR-04);
- a|aa|...|a^499, two of them in one document, and a long-tail shape the measure refuses, with a compile-budget assertion (CR-05);
- a TotalAlloc bound for x{0,1000} and the pattern memory cases of TestPatternProgramMemory (CR-09).

**Contract.** None (the refusals use the approved pattern caps).

### GC-07. Dynamic-scope work per step ignores anchor-name bytes and the decision wrapper's frames

Severity: blocker. Members: 4.

**Root cause.** working.go:1079-1081 charges evals x frames x scopeUnits, a flat 4 units per step, whatever the anchor's length. resolveDynamicAnchor (validator.go:781-791) hashes or compares the whole name at every scope step, and validateRefs (validator.go:805-808) compares it once more per evaluation. The scan also starts frames at 0 (working.go:995) although the decision wrapper adds three scopes that checkCycle and every dynamic resolution walk.

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VAL-46 | validate | V1 | gap | blocker | $dynamicRef resolution: map lookup or compare of the whole anchor name at every scope step, plus sch.DynamicAnchor == dref.Anchor once per evaluation | - |
| VAL-65 | validate | V1 | partial | minor | the decision pass's wrapper scopes (decide, its not, its $ref) are walked by checkCycle at the root value and by every $dynamicRef/$recursiveRef resolution, but the scan starts frames at 0 | - |
| REFS-29 | refs | V1 | gap | blocker | $dynamicRef dynamic-scope resolution: anchor name length (round-9 finding 3, open) | - |
| CX-05 | cross | V1 | gap | blocker | $dynamicRef x $dynamicAnchor name length x dynamic scope depth | - |

**Fix for the class.** Record the longest $dynamicAnchor or $dynamicRef anchor name (per state, or in workFlags) and charge evals x frames x (scopeUnits + anchorLen/nameHashBytes), plus evals x anchorLen/nameHashBytes for the static comparison. Start frames at decideFrames and add them to the root value's cycle-check chain.

**Tests.**

- TestWorkCharges rows with 1-, 8-, 808- and 100,000-byte anchors pinning the per-byte difference, and a BenchmarkWorkModel long-anchor case (VAL-46, REFS-29, CX-05);
- the 'dynamic reference' row restated with the wrapper frames, and a row for a $dynamicRef at the root value (VAL-65).

**Contract.** None. The alternative (bound anchor names with RZ-CFG-005) needs a contract request against 07 req 75 and R-77.

### GC-08. Peak-heap test assertions sample garbage, so they depend on machine load

Severity: blocker. Members: 2.

**Root cause.** peakHeap (working_test.go:200-223) samples HeapInuse every 100 us at GOGC 10 and allows a fixed 1 MiB gcSlack. Shapes that churn garbage (allOf branches copying unevaluated maps) exceed the slack when the collector lags under CPU contention, so tests fail intermittently on the Go 1.26 floor and on go1.27.1 under load, while the live memory stays within the charge.

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VAL-44 | validate | V2 | partial | major | TestWorkingSetCoversPeak reliability for small reservations (sampled heap peak of the copies) | - |
| CX-13 | cross | D | gap | blocker | Tests asserting a sampled heap peak (peakHeap) are load-dependent, unevaluated x allOf branches first | - |

**Fix for the class.** Replace every sampled peak with a deterministic live-heap measurement: an x-probe hook that runs runtime.GC and reads HeapAlloc at the deepest point of each branch, as decisionTree already does, or TotalAlloc bounds for the transient copies. Keep the 413 regressions without a peak comparison where the probe cannot reach.

**Tests.**

Rewrite each peakHeap user: TestWorkingSetCoversPeak, TestHandleUnevaluatedBranches, TestHandlePatternFit, TestHandleBacktrackCharge, TestHandleAcceptsLargeBodies, TestHandleBoundsMatchedItems, TestHandleBoundsUnevaluatedMemory, TestHandleBoundsValidationMemory and TestPathStates. Acceptance: the Go 1.26 floor (GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.8 GOEXPERIMENT=jsonv2) and the CGO_ENABLED=0 suites pass with -count=10 in three concurrent processes beside busy loops.

**Contract.** None.

### GC-09. Compile work from counts times counts, outside any budget or benchmark

Severity: major. Members: 10.

**Root cause.** MaxSubschemas bounds each count, but the library and WP-51's analysis do work proportional to products of counts: resource scans per position and per reference, anchors times dynamic anchors, $dynamicRef edge lists, dependencies array members the walk does not count, number literals parsed at compile, declared names in the state builder, and compile-time name matching. None of these products is in a compile budget or in BenchmarkCompileAnalysisWorstCase, so 07 F6's 1 s and doc.go's figures are unproven for them.
