|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CMP-13 | compile | C1 | partial | minor | Resource bookkeeping scans with short ids: per-position baseRes scan, duplicate-id scan, resolve() scan over resources | - |
| CMP-14 | compile | C1 | partial | minor | Eager compile of every $dynamicAnchor of a resource (anchors x dynamicAnchors scan) | - |
| CMP-47 | compile | C1 | partial | minor | $dynamicRef: WP-51 analysis edge lists (each $dynamicRef to every schema with the same $dynamicAnchor) | - |
| REFS-21 | refs | C1 | partial | major | Number of $id resources: linear resource scans per collected position and per reference with a URL part (every reference when the root declares $id) | - |
| REFS-50 | refs | C1 | partial | minor | Name-list members of dependencies: positions the library collects (AllProp) and meta-checks one by one, accepted, not counted by the walk | - |
| REFS-51 | refs | C1 | partial | minor | Dynamic anchors of a resource: enqueued at every resource-root compile with a slices.Contains scan, and duplicated on re-collection | - |
| VLU-18 | values | C1 | partial | minor | Compile cost of documents full of literals at the number bounds (numVal, intVal, meta-check isInteger/minimum parses, boundsFailing Cmp, kept rationals) | - |
| CX-19 | cross | C1 | partial | minor | Long declared names x compile-time states (byName maps, memberEntries search) | - |
| CX-30 | cross | C1 | partial | minor | Numerous $dynamicAnchor names in one resource x the library's eager dynamic-anchor compile | - |
| CR-23 | cross | C2 | partial | minor | Compile-time name matching transient memory (job stack, NFA queues and add recursion on declared names up to ~256 KB) | GC-11 |

**Fix for the class.** Add count terms to GC-03's compile budget (or show each product stays within F6 at its bound): resources x (positions + references) (CMP-13, REFS-21); anchors x dynamic anchors x name bytes (CMP-14, REFS-51, CX-30); the sum of $dynamicRef edge-list lengths (CMP-47); dependencies array members counted as positions (REFS-50); declared name bytes in stateBudget (CX-19). Put every such shape at its bound, and the number-heavy documents (VLU-18) and compile-time matching (CR-23), in analysisCases and the combined worst case, and restate doc.go's figures.

**Tests.**

For each term, a deterministic count test (the budget charge for the shape and a refusal one past the bound), plus an analysisCases row:
- 4,000 $id resources with and without a root $id, plus 2,000 URL references (CMP-13, REFS-21);
- 4,096 dynamic anchors with long common-prefix names in one resource (CMP-14, REFS-51, CX-30);
- 2,000 $dynamicRef x 2,000 same-named anchors (CMP-47);
- 20,000 dependencies members of ["a"] (REFS-50);
- integer keywords at 1e1000, bounds at 1e-1000 and 128-byte literals, const and enum numbers, with a kept-memory assertion (VLU-18);
- a 120,000-byte declared name reached by 1,993 states (CX-19);
- a long declared name against an alternation-heavy patternProperties pattern, with a compile-peak assertion (CR-23).

**Contract.** Refusals from count terms join GC-03's contract request.

### GC-10. The raw config.schema is decoded in full before the size bound

Severity: major. Members: 2.

**Root cause.** prepare (schema.go:195-213) decodes the raw document with jsonval.Decode and no MaxCost, then checks the canonical size; the raw size is bounded only by the configuration loader's 64 MiB source default.

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CMP-16 | compile | C1 | gap | major | prepare decodes the whole raw config.schema before the 256 KiB canonical bound | - |
| CMP-17 | compile | C2 | gap | major | prepare decodes the whole raw config.schema: peak memory | - |

**Fix for the class.** Decode with a jsonval MaxCost derived from MaxSchemaBytes (for example (jsonval.MaxCostPerByte + MaxNumberBytes) x MaxSchemaBytes), or pre-scan the raw bytes counting canonical bytes with an early stop.

**Tests.**

- a 12 MB raw document of 4,000,000 empty objects and a 63 MB one refused with the size finding and bounded allocations through CompileSchema and Check (CMP-16, CMP-17);
- the boundary at MaxSchemaBytes on both sides, and a whitespace-heavy raw document that canonicalizes under the bound still accepted.

**Contract.** None.

### GC-11. Go regexp matcher costs not derived from the compiled program

Severity: major. Members: 5.

**Root cause.** The pattern transient is 32 KiB plus 64 bytes per instruction (working.go:81-85), charged once, and the work is per input byte. Go's regexp allocates NFA queues by matchPool bucket (regexp.go:224-257) plus threads per rune instruction, recurses in add() along InstAlt.Out chains on the goroutine stack (exec.go:317-376), zeroes queues on first use, runs strings.Index for literal prefixes, and keeps machines and bit states in sync.Pool after the request ends.

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CR-15 | cross | V2 | gap | major | NFA simulation memory: run queues sized by the matchPool bucket, and threads | - |
| CR-16 | cross | V2 | gap | major | NFA add() recursion depth on the goroutine stack (chains of InstAlt.Out links: plain alternations, non-greedy optionals and stars, (?U)) | GC-15 |
| CR-18 | cross | V1 | partial | minor | Regex matching CPU per match: first-use allocation of machines and bit states | - |
| CR-19 | cross | V1 | partial | minor | Regex literal-prefix search (strings.Index) in unanchored matches | - |
| CR-20 | cross | V2 | partial | minor | Matchers and bit states retained in sync.Pool (after the request releases its reservation, and across pools within one request) | - |

**Fix for the class.** Compute per compiled program, at compile, in patternCost and backtrackProg (so states keep their per-path selection): NFA queue bytes as 40 x the mpool bucket (len(Inst) in the large pool) plus about 56 bytes per rune instruction a queue can hold; add()'s recursion depth as the longest InstAlt.Out chain through empty-width steps, charged at measured frame bytes x 2 for the doubling and copy (or refused past a depth bound); the first-use zeroing as work once per bucket per pass; the literal-prefix Index work. State the pool retention and its bound in doc.go 'Working set'.

**Tests.**

- bucket edges 128/129, 512/513, 2048/2049 and 16384/16385 and thread-heavy programs, measured after clearing the pools; a Handle case at 64 KiB with a short string; TestHandleMatcherCharge restated against the library (CR-15);
- 1,000 and 2,700 plain alternations, 1,000 and 8,000 non-greedy optionals, (?U) stars and greedy controls, in a child process with debug.SetMaxStack as TestStackWithinCharge does (CR-16);
- a BenchmarkPatternModel case run after runtime.GC (CR-18);
- unanchored long-literal-prefix patterns with near-miss inputs in BenchmarkPatternModel and TestHandleWorkBound (CR-19);
- retained heap after Handle (one runtime.GC) for the largest NFA and job-stack shapes, and a request matching a backtracker and an NFA pattern on two members that both pass (CR-20).

**Contract.** Only if add() depth is refused rather than charged (RZ-CFG-005, contract request against R-77).

### GC-12. What the compiled Schema keeps beyond the stated figures

Severity: major. Members: 4.

**Root cause.** doc.go:113-121 (about 16 MB; decision 15 about 9 MB) rests on positions x 2.4 KB and 40 bytes per regex instruction. The compiled Schema also keeps the decoded enum and const values and a big.Rat per numeric keyword (CMP-54), annotation values validation never reads (CMP-55), the Inst slice's spare capacity and literal-prefix copies of each regexp (CR-11), and each regexp's expression text (CR-28).

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CMP-54 | compile | C3 | partial | minor | enum/const values and numeric rationals kept by the compiled Schema | - |
| CMP-55 | compile | C3 | gap | major | Annotation values kept although validation never reads them (examples, default, title, description, $comment; readOnly, writeOnly, deprecated booleans) | - |
| CR-11 | cross | C3 | gap | major | Memory a compiled regexp keeps per measured instruction (Inst slice capacity, literal prefix copies, literal rune capacity) | - |
| CR-28 | cross | C3 | partial | minor | Compiled regexp keeps a copy of its expression text (regexp.Regexp.expr = noOnePass+expr) and capture-name table, outside the measure | - |

**Fix for the class.** One kept-memory model and test. Clear the annotation fields WP-51 never reads after compile (Examples, Default, Title, Description, Comment) on every reachable Schema. Count the decoded size of enum and const values and the rationals in a kept budget, or bound them. Model a regexp's kept bytes exactly: the capacity append reaches for n 40-byte Insts (size classes), twice the UTF-8 bytes of Prog.Prefix when unanchored, the literal runes' capacity, and the expression text rounded to its size class. Restate doc.go's and R-77's kept figures.

**Tests.**

Heap after GC with the Schema live:
- 256 KB of one-member objects in enum and in const, and rationals at exponent 1000 over 4,094 positions (CMP-54);
- 256 KB of examples and of default, a 250 KB title, and the combined case plus 200 KB of examples within 16 MiB (CMP-55);
- a sweep of run lengths 1 to 2,100 for ASCII, 2-, 3- and 4-byte runes, anchored and not, and classes, kept <= measure x 40 per pattern, plus the document cases at MaxDocumentInstructions (CR-11);
- flag-only (?i) x 60,000 and long capture-name patterns against measure x 40 (CR-28).

**Contract.** Restated kept figures go to R-77 and 07 req 90 (Open question already recorded by decision 15).

### GC-13. The number model approximates where the library parses and hashes

Severity: major. Members: 4.

**Root cause.** 07 req 80 and decision 14 refuse a body number only where the library would parse it. The scan applies the bound and the charges below every possible parse site (parsesOf, noteNumbers, dimHash, b.comparing) instead of the library's actual ones: validator.go:99 short-circuits when number is listed, validator.go:343 skips arrays of one item, and equals returns before parsing on a length or kind mismatch (util.go:290, 305, 322). For uniqueItems decimals past 64 bits the collider count is a crude estimate (working.go:1390-1429), and smallRat and hashColliders are untested for exponent forms.

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VLU-07 | values | W | gap | minor | type list holding both integer and number: the library never parses (contains(number) short-circuits) but WP-51 counts a parse and applies the number bound | - |
| VLU-11 | values | W | gap | minor | Number bound applied where the library never parses: uniqueItems arrays of 0 or 1 item and pairwise arrays where a number never meets another number at the same place; composite const/enum values whose shape differs from the body (length or member-count mismatch) | - |
| VLU-12 | values | W | gap | major | uniqueItems hash-collision count for decimals whose rational exceeds 64 bits (\|exponent\| above about 19 or more than about 19 digits) | - |
| VLU-13 | values | V1 | partial | minor | uniqueItems collision model for decimals within 64 bits (hashColliders, smallRat, smooth): exponent forms, zero, -0, shifts that overflow | - |

**Fix for the class.** Compute parse and hash sites exactly: count the integer type only when the list does not also hold number; set dimHash only for arrays of more than one item, and for pairwise arrays only on paths where two items hold numbers; set b.comparing only below values whose length or member count matches some compared const or enum value; compute each number's hash key bytes (numerator then denominator) during the scan into a reused buffer, or generalize hashColliders to big integers, and charge the real bucket sizes.

**Tests.**

- TestHandleNumberPaths rows: [integer,number] and [number,integer] accepted, [integer,string] and [integer] refused at 1e1001 (VLU-07);
- uniqueItems [x], [x,"a"] and [[x],["a"]] accepted, [x,1] and 21 numeric items refused; const and enum with mismatched shapes expecting the /const or /enum detail; the existing one-item row corrected (VLU-11; keeps VLU-10's hash and pairwise paths pinned);
- TestHandleMeasuredFit rows for 2,000 distinct decimals at e-300, e23 and 22 digits accepted; big decimals that truly collide still charged; a scan allocation test (VLU-12);
- a smallRat and hashColliders table against big.Rat for e, E, e+ and e- exponents, zero, -0, 0e5, shifts that overflow 64 bits and parts over 8 bytes; the groups loop extended to exponent forms (VLU-13).

**Contract.** None (this brings the code to 07 req 80 as decided).

### GC-14. Values outside the decoder's data model through the exported M2 API

Severity: major. Members: 1.

**Root cause.** Handle decodes with jsonval, so only map[string]any, []any, string, json.Number, bool and nil reach the library. Schema.Validate and Schema.ValidateWithin accept any Go value: invalid types are read as 'valid' (validate.go:86-88), arbitrary json.Number text panics in math/big or is accepted as a number, and float64 and int parses are not charged.

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VLU-23 | values | P | gap | major | Values outside the decoder's data model given to the exported M2 API (Schema.Validate, Schema.ValidateWithin): float64/int/NaN/Inf, other Go types, json.Number text that is not an RFC 8259 literal | - |

**Fix for the class.** The scan rejects, before any library call, every value outside the data model and every json.Number that is not an RFC 8259 literal (jsonval's grammar), returning not ok (never valid) or an error, with recursion bounded by maxNesting for cyclic values. Update the ValidateWithin documentation for M2 callers.

**Tests.**

A table over float32 and float64 (NaN, +Inf, -Inf, 1.5), every int and uint width, []string, map[string]string, struct, pointer and json.RawMessage, and json.Number 'abc', '1e', '', 'Inf', '0x10' and '1/3', nested and at the root, for Validate and ValidateWithin; a cyclic map.

**Contract.** The exported API's documented behaviour changes (M2 callers); no RZ code.

### GC-15. The stack charge has no absolute ceiling below the Go runtime's maximum

Severity: major. Members: 2.

**Root cause.** The path (stack) term is bounded only relative to the working-set limit, 8 x maxRequestBodyBytes, which has no maximum (pkg/config/v1alpha1/gateway.go:153-155). In-place chains reach MaxEvaluations x 64 levels of library frames, and growing past the runtime's maximum goroutine stack is a fatal, unrecoverable error.

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VAL-53 | validate | P | partial | major | recursion depth / goroutine stack (validate, validateSelf, validateVal, objValidate frames; a propertyNames sub-validation on top) | GC-11 |
| CX-12 | cross | P | partial | minor | Nesting x in-place chains x Go's stack ceiling (a stack past the 512 MiB step throws 'stack overflow') | GC-11 |

**Fix for the class.** Cap the path term at an absolute ceiling well below the runtime maximum, independent of the configured limits, and answer 413 above it; include GC-11's add() depth in the same stack term; state the margin in doc.go.

**Tests.**

- a TestStackWithinCharge-style child process with debug.SetMaxStack(256 MiB) at the largest admitted path for each chain kind ($ref, allOf, not, $dynamicRef, dependentSchemas) at 4,095 evaluations x 64 levels, with a large-alternation pattern at the leaf (VAL-53, CX-12);
- a unit test that the cap applies at very large configured limits (1 GiB).

**Contract.** A new 413 condition: contract note against R-77 and 07 reqs 80 to 82.

### GC-17. Work-model terms that no test proves

Severity: major. Members: 10.

**Root cause.** The charges were written from code reading and are checked by rows that cancel the term or never reach it. Mutants that remove a term pass the whole suite: numberWork's exponent term (VLU-02), parsesOf's const branch (VLU-08), compareWork's string bytes (VLU-27), sortUnits for hashed objects (VLU-29, VAL-27), the 'every' node's parse (VLU-56), queueBound's scan charge (CR-21) and the contains per-loop list (CX-25). workingSetCases has no uniqueItems case (VAL-28), and the numeric bounds have no term for the bound's digits (VAL-10).

| VAL-28 | validate | V2 | partial | minor | uniqueItems hashed memory (duplicates map, writeHash name slices) | - |
| VLU-02 | values | V1 | partial | major | Work of one number parse and comparison at the literal bounds (numberWork) for minimum, maximum, exclusiveMinimum, exclusiveMaximum, the integer type, const/enum numbers and uniqueItems pairs | - |

| VLU-29 | values | V1 | partial | minor | uniqueItems hashing of objects: member names sorted (slices.Sort) and hashed, each value looked up by name | - |

| CR-21 | cross | C1 | partial | minor | WP-51's own compile-time pattern analysis (queue exploration, class representatives, held alternations) | - |
| CX-25 | cross | V2 | partial | minor | contains x minContains/maxContains x items x nesting (matched indexes listed while looping and kept in errors) | GC-08 |

**Fix for the class.** A term inventory: for each term of workWeight, scanWeight, enter, the bodyScan methods and the regex analysis budgets, one exact-unit row (TestWorkCharges, TestWorkingSetCharges, TestQueueBoundBudget) whose expected value moves with the term, checked by mutation, and a BenchmarkWorkModel case for each cost shape. Add a bound-digits term for the numeric bounds (VAL-10) if the new benchmark cases exceed the documented 0.8 ns per unit.

**Tests.**

- exponent literals (1e-1000, and a 128-byte literal at e1000) under minimum, maximum, exclusiveMinimum, exclusiveMaximum, the integer type, const and enum, and BenchmarkWorkModel cases against short and 1000-digit bounds (VLU-02, VAL-10);
- a top-level const number refused at 1e1001, a const string not refused, an enum of mixed strings and numbers, and a 'const number' charge row (VLU-08);

- workingSetCases with a large hashed uniqueItems array of distinct objects and of colliding arrays (VAL-28);
- a 21-item numeric uniqueItems row pinning (1 + comparisons) x numberWork, and benchmark cases for 2,000 distinct 128-byte integers and exponent literals (VLU-56);
- TestQueueBoundBudget asserting that the sparse and alternation cases spend their budget (CR-21);
- nested arrays each under contains matching every item, valid and failing, measured with GC-08's deterministic method (CX-25);
- round-9 minor 6: an exact stateBudget delta of next() with and without pattern programs (G1), and a reachMerger case with two equal-size programs of different held counts (F1);
- VLU-01's note: TestHandleNumberPaths rows for maximum, exclusiveMinimum and exclusiveMaximum with 1e1001 and 1e1000001 bodies, so each refusal is pinned and not only its charge.

### GC-19. Compile diagnostics: library errors and WP-51 fallbacks are not deterministic findings at the right pointer

Severity: minor. Members: 8.

**Root cause.** When the walk does not pre-empt a library compile error, compileIssues (schema.go:406-423) returns it raw at pointer '', and the library picks which error in map order. Fallbacks format whole ValidationError trees before the message cap; checkApart has no fallback when metaIssues returns nothing; message() rewrites the operator's own $id when it equals a WP-51 URL; and the 64-level decode limit surfaces as 'not valid JSON'.

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CMP-43 | compile | D | gap | minor | $id: an invalid URI makes the library fail with ParseIDError, chosen in map-iteration order | - |
| CMP-44 | compile | W | gap | minor | $id: wrong location for a refused $id | - |
| CMP-77 | compile | W | gap | minor | NEW (r9 minor, still open). A schema nested deeper than 64 JSON levels is refused as 'schema is not valid JSON: jsonval: nesting too deep', an undocumented rule with a misleading message | - |
| CMP-78 | compile | P | gap | minor | NEW (r9 minor, still open). checkApart has no fallback finding when metaIssues returns none for a failing position | - |
| REFS-14 | refs | D | gap | minor | Library-ordered findings when the walk does not pre-empt the library's error ($recursiveRef, base mismatch, unparseable $id on a walked route) | GC-01, GC-02 |
| REFS-37 | refs | W | gap | minor | Unparseable $id | - |
| REFS-43 | refs | S | partial | minor | WP-51's own resources and URLs (policy.json, check.json, decide.json) as $id or reference in the document | - |
| VLU-39 | values | C2 | partial | minor | Fallback text of compile errors: err.Error() of SchemaValidationError (whole meta-error tree via ValidationError.Error/display), ParseURLError, InvalidJsonPointerError, JSONPointerNotFoundError, InvalidRegexError | - |

**Fix for the class.** The walk pre-empts every library compile error class at its own pointer, in walk order: an $id url.Parse rejects at /.../$id, and (through GC-01 and GC-02) missing anchors and pointers, duplicates, invalid regexes and unsupported vocabularies. Any residual library error maps to one deterministic finding at the nearest walked position, built from the first leaf's kindMessage, never by formatting a tree; checkApart gets the same fallback. message() quotes operator values verbatim. For depth, either decode with the configuration's depth limit or state the 64-level rule with its own message. Add a FuzzCompileSchema property: two compiles give identical issues and no issue carries a raw library message.

**Tests.**

- {'$defs':{'a':{'$id':'%%'},'b':{'$id':'%zz'},'c':{'$id':'http://[::1'}}} gives the same findings at each /$id pointer over 30 runs and through Check (CMP-43, CMP-44, REFS-37);
- the three REFS-14 shapes give identical issues over 40 compiles once GC-01 and GC-02 land (REFS-14);
- 32 and 33 nested properties levels, on both sides of the limit, with the stated message (CMP-77);
- checkApart with metaIssues returning nothing (CMP-78), and the meta-error fallback forced with an injected location outside baseURL, with an allocation bound (VLU-39);
- policy.json, check.json and decide.json as root and nested $id and as reference, with messages quoting the operator's $id verbatim (REFS-43).

**Contract.** Keeping the 64-level rule needs it stated in 07 req 75's contract request (round-9 minor 5).

### GC-20. Semantic deviations of the library and Go regexp that are not documented or pinned

Severity: minor. Members: 6.

**Root cause.** Some results differ from what an operator writing draft 2020-12 with ECMA-262 patterns expects, and neither doc.go nor a test records them: propertyNames validates each name with a fresh scope, so a $dynamicRef there resolves without the outer resources; compilePattern's '(?:)' prefix adds a nesting level, so a valid RE2 pattern 999 groups deep is refused with a misleading message; the regexp recursion at Go's height limit is untested; \p classes and folds follow the toolchain's Unicode version (15.0.0 on the floor, 17.0.0 on go1.27.1); and RE2's \s and '.' differ from ECMA-262's.

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VAL-24 | validate | W | partial | minor | propertyNames resets the dynamic scope (fresh scope per name) | - |
| CX-06 | cross | W | gap | minor | $dynamicRef x propertyNames (fresh validator per member name loses the dynamic scope) | - |
| CR-07 | cross | P | partial | minor | Regex recursion over the parse tree (parser, Simplify, syntax.Compile, minInputLen, WP-51 programInsts/programNodes) | - |
| CR-08 | cross | W | gap | minor | compilePattern's empty-group prefix (?:) at Go's height limit | - |
| CR-26 | cross | D | partial | minor | Unicode version and script names of the toolchain's regexp tables | - |
| CR-27 | cross | W | partial | minor | ECMA-262 vs RE2 meaning of syntax both accept (\s, ., \S, folds) | - |

**Fix for the class.** Document each deviation in doc.go and the operator docs, pin each with a test, and fix the one WP-51 causes: add the one-pass guard without adding height (a program-level Nop, or the empty match inside the top concat when the tree is at height 1000), or refuse at the walk with a clear RE2-limit finding. For propertyNames, document the deviation (or, by contract request, refuse a $dynamicRef reachable from propertyNames whose anchor is also defined outside that subtree).

**Tests.**

- the propertyNames $dynamicRef case and its in-place control, through Handle (VAL-24, CX-06);
- 998-, 999- and 1000-deep captures and non-capturing groups, and (?:x{0,1000}) nests with a stack bound, in TestCompilePatternMatches and the FuzzCompilePattern seeds (CR-07, CR-08);
- the measure's table sizes equal the running parser's, and a Unicode 16 script compiles only when unicode.Version >= 16 (CR-26);
- \s against U+00A0, U+FEFF and \v, and '.' against \r and U+2028 (CR-27).

**Contract.** Only the propertyNames refusal option needs a contract request (07 req 76).

### GC-21. Format assertion reaches request bodies on library-only routes, and the asserted format 'regex' calls WP-51's compile-time regexp cache at request time

Severity: blocker. Members: 9.

**Root cause.** Under draft 2020-12 the library asserts format when the resource's dialect has the format-assertion vocabulary (objcompiler.go:436-445), and the dialect comes from the resource's own $schema (roots.go:136-146; loader.go:196-218; draft.go:181-223). WP-51 keeps format an annotation by two means only: it never calls AssertFormat (schema.go:261-265), and recordOnce refuses every $schema other than Draft, but only at walked positions; the inspector checks DraftVersion (inspect.go:169-171), which such a dialect passes. On a library-only route (a $recursiveRef target, GC-01; a base-mismatch target, GC-02) a resource whose $schema is the bundled https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/meta/format-assertion, or the policy document itself with a root $vocabulary listing format-assertion, compiles with Format set. For 'regex', Format.Validate is roots.regexpEngine.validate (objcompiler.go:188-192), bound to WP-51's regexpCache.compile (schema.go:383-403): a plain map, written without a lock, that every compiled Schema then keeps. validator.go:136-142 calls it on every string evaluated there, because Schema.Validate passes no regexp engine. So concurrent requests write the map and the process dies with an unrecoverable fatal error (CC-01); every distinct body string is compiled and kept for the life of the Schema (CC-02); that compile and the other validators' work are outside the work and working-set models, which count Format only as an error unit (working.go:440, :632) (CC-03); and format is asserted on bodies against 07 req 75 and doc.go:22, while the vocabulary subset also drops the validation and applicator keywords (CC-04). The rows that state format is never asserted or that the Schema is never mutated (VLU-41, CMP-58, VAL-13, CX-28, VAL-68) hold at walked positions only. Content keywords stay annotations on every route: the library asserts them only under the AssertContent compiler flag (objcompiler.go:239, :305), which WP-51 never sets.

| Row | Area | Cost | Status | Sev. | Feature | Also needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CC-01 | completeness | P | gap | blocker | Format assertion through a dialect's vocabularies at library-only positions: a resource on a library-only route ($recursiveRef target, or a base-mismatch target) whose $schema gives it the format-assertion vocabulary. Then the library's request-time format 'regex' check calls WP-51's compile-time regexpCache, a plain map | GC-01, GC-02 |
| CC-02 | completeness | C3 | gap | blocker | Request-time regex format: every distinct body string is compiled and kept in regexpCache for the life of the Schema | - |
| CC-03 | completeness | V2 | gap | major | Request-time format work outside the working-set and work models: body strings compiled as regexps, and format validators that split strings per separator | - |
| CC-04 | completeness | W | gap | major | Format asserted on bodies, against doc.go:22 and 07, on library-only routes; the vocabulary subset also drops the validation and applicator keywords there | GC-01, GC-02 |
| CMP-58 | compile | V1 | partial | major | format keyword (annotation only) | - |
| VAL-13 | validate | V1 | partial | major | format assertion | - |
| VAL-68 | validate | D | partial | blocker | concurrent validation over one compiled Schema (shared root and decide, shared regexps) | - |
| VLU-41 | values | W | partial | major | format keyword (all format.go validators, regex, unknown names) stays an annotation on bodies | - |
| CX-28 | cross | W | partial | major | format and content keywords x any other keyword | - |

**Fix for the class.** (1) After compile, the inspector refuses with RZ-CFG-005, at the keyword's pointer, any compiled Schema whose Format is set, and any whose ContentEncoding, ContentMediaType or ContentSchema is set (a backstop: AssertContent is never set), because 07 req 75 makes format and the content keywords annotations on every route. (2) The regexp engine WP-51 gives the library is frozen once compileWalked returns: a later call fails without caching, and no compiled Schema keeps a reference to the cache's map (for example a per-compile engine value whose state is dropped when compile ends; compiled Pattern values keep only their own \*compiledPattern). (3) GC-01 and GC-02 close the routes, so the walk's $schema rule applies at every position the library compiles and no dialect other than Draft is ever compiled. (4) Restate doc.go:22 and the rows' claims: format and content stay annotations because of (1) and (3), not only because AssertFormat and AssertContent are unset. Asserting format would need its own work and working-set charges and an ADR.

**Tests.**

Deterministic only: no heap sampling and no timing (GC-08).
- TestCompileSchemaFindings rows through CompileSchema, Check and Factory.Build with a spy loader, refused with RZ-CFG-005 at the format keyword and no load: format regex, email, hostname, date-time, uuid and json-pointer at a $recursiveRef target whose $schema is policy.json with a root $vocabulary listing format-assertion; the same at a base-mismatch target; the bundled meta/format-assertion as $schema with no root $vocabulary, with a sibling type keyword (CC-01, CC-04, VLU-41, CMP-58, VAL-13, CX-28); these rows pass on (1) alone and again after GC-01 and GC-02, where the walk refuses them first at /$schema;
- TestInspectorBackstop rows with a compiled Schema whose Format, ContentEncoding, ContentMediaType or ContentSchema is injected, each refused at its keyword (CC-01);
- an engine test: a call counter shows no engine call after compileWalked returns, and a call on the frozen engine fails and leaves the cache unchanged (CC-02, CC-03);
- a -race test running Handle from 8 goroutines on a compiled filter whose schema has patterns and patternProperties at several positions, asserting the engine is never called after compile, beside TestHandleConcurrent (CC-01, VAL-68);
- the CC-02 and CC-03 shapes (2,000 distinct body strings, a 180 KB body string under format regex, the json-pointer format on a 1 MiB body): their schemas are refused at compile, so no request reaches the engine; asserted by the refusal and the engine counter, never by measuring (CC-02, CC-03);
- a 'regex' format row in TestCompileSchemaAccepts at a walked position, still an annotation that accepts '(' (VLU-41's optional row).

**Contract.** None: 07 req 75 already requires every $schema to be exactly Draft and format and the content keywords to be annotations; the refusal is a backstop of that rule with RZ-CFG-005.

### 4.4 Limits of the audit and leads

- Bounded rows proven by reading only. The validate verifier checked VAL-01, VAL-05 to VAL-10, VAL-14 to VAL-16, VAL-18 to VAL-20, VAL-22, VAL-31, VAL-39 to VAL-45, VAL-51 to VAL-56 and VAL-58 to VAL-64 by code reading, without mutants. The bounded ones among them (VAL-01, VAL-05, VAL-06, VAL-07, VAL-08, VAL-09, VAL-14, VAL-15, VAL-16, VAL-18, VAL-19, VAL-20, VAL-22, VAL-31, VAL-39, VAL-40, VAL-42, VAL-43, VAL-45, VAL-51, VAL-52, VAL-54, VAL-55, VAL-56, VAL-58, VAL-60, VAL-61, VAL-62, VAL-64) rest on the named tests as read; the fix pass mutation-checks them with GC-17's term inventory.
- Hostile-input probes the validate verifier did not run: VAL-46, VAL-49 and VAL-53 rest on code reading and the round-9 records. REFS-29 re-measured VAL-46's shape, and VLU-16 and VLU-51 probed VAL-49's.
- No probe or mutant ran on the Go 1.26.8 floor (the completeness check's probes were built with go1.27.1 too); CR-26 shows that Unicode-dependent results differ between the floor and go1.27.1. GC-08's acceptance run covers the floor.
- Leads the verifiers named without a row, which the completeness check's report does not take up, so they stay leads for the fix pass: which of errNestingTooDeep and errWorkTooLarge a body that is both too deep and over the work limit gets (both 413 RZ-RT-003; values verifier, D); the combined worst case of many $id resources, dynamic anchors and dependencies lists together (refs verifier; belongs in GC-09's analysisCases); the kept memory of the long-target and nested-$id shapes (compile verifier, not measured); the within-request sum of matchers from several pools (CR-20, not reproduced).
- Format validators (corrected by the completeness check). At walked positions only uri, uri-reference and regex run, on schema strings in the meta-check, and no format validator runs on bodies. On a library-only route whose dialect has format-assertion every format.go validator runs on body strings (CC-01 to CC-04, GC-21); the check probed regex, email, hostname, date-time, uuid and json-pointer, and the other validators are covered by signature and by GC-21's refusal, which does not depend on the format name.
- Library code covered by signature only: the bodies of kind/kind.go LocalizedString, content.go and vocab.go, which are never active on any route (AssertContent and RegisterVocabulary are never called).

## 5. Open round-9 findings mapped to rows and classes

Round 9 (runs/w3-s2-wp51-r9.json, run wf_03263e09-542) left four blockers and eight minors open. Numbering is 1-based in the order of the record's findings and minors arrays, as the rows cite them.

| Round-9 item | Severity | Rows | Class | How the audit extends it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Finding 1: $recursiveRef targets escape the rule walk (walk.go:346) | blocker | Core: CMP-33, REFS-10, CX-02. Its six shapes: (1) panic CMP-34, CMP-22, VLU-51, CX-22; (2) number bounds VLU-52, VLU-16, REFS-11; (3) pattern bounds CMP-36, CMP-23, CMP-24, CX-27; (4) MaxSubschemas REFS-11, CX-21; (5) loader CMP-35, REFS-12, CX-03; (6) the round-8 additionalItems fix bypassed CMP-34, REFS-13, VAL-49 | GC-01 | Adds the run-time side, where no test mentions $recursiveRef (VAL-47, REFS-31 to REFS-34), $recursiveAnchor (VAL-48, REFS-15, CX-04), the meta re-check bytes (CX-29), the static enforcement left undocumented (CMP-37) and the false doc.go claim (REFS-25). The same bypass exists without $recursiveRef: base mismatch, a late $id and an ancestor target's $id (GC-02: CMP-68 to CMP-71, REFS-01 to REFS-09, REFS-47). The completeness check found three more consequences on these routes: format asserted on bodies, with the format 'regex' check calling WP-51's compile-time regexp cache from every request (CC-01 to CC-04, GC-21); older-draft meta-checks reaching the writeHash panic through enum (CC-05); and hash collisions in the meta-check's uniqueItems (CC-06), both in GC-02. |
| Finding 2: long member names make the library's compile-queue search quadratic | blocker | CMP-02, CMP-03, CMP-04, REFS-18, REFS-20, VLU-30, VLU-55, CX-17 | GC-03 | The root cause is string bytes times uses, and the queue is one multiplier among several: per value, schema or not (CMP-05, CMP-76, CMP-72 to CMP-75, REFS-22), per reference (CMP-11, CMP-12, CMP-64, CMP-65, VLU-54), per resource (CMP-66, CMP-67) and per clone (CMP-09). A budget over positions alone, as round 9 proposed, would not bound these. Index aliases (GC-04) repeat the whole cost under new spellings. |
| Finding 3: the dynamic-scope work charge ignores $dynamicAnchor name length | blocker | VAL-46, REFS-29, CX-05 | GC-07 | Re-measured on the working tree (REFS-29: a 100,000-byte anchor runs at 126 ns per charged unit against 2.2 for a 1-byte one). Adds the decision wrapper's three uncharged scope frames (VAL-65). |
| Finding 4: load-dependent TestWorkingSetCoversPeak cases | blocker | VAL-44, CX-13 | GC-08 | Wider than round 9 stated: every peakHeap user samples garbage, and TestHandleUnevaluatedBranches failed in 2 of 3 concurrent runs on go1.27.1 (CX-13). The new CX-25 test depends on this fix. |
| Minor 1: identifies() makes an unreferenced additionalItems subtree live | minor | CMP-27, CX-23 | GC-02 | The over-approximation side of the walk-versus-library liveness class. |
| Minor 2: comments not updated for the MinScaledLimit floor (filter.go:55-72, doc.go Work bound, ValidateWithin) | minor | none | none | WP-51 documentation, not a library path; carried as recorded. |
| Minor 3: integer keywords above int64 wrap | minor | CMP-56, VLU-17 | GC-05 | Raised to blocker by VLU-50: a wrapped negative maxLength aliases the -1 'absent' sentinel, so the decision pass holds 8.95 MB while 104 B is charged. |
| Minor 4: the job-stack window binds for common patterns with two held alternations | minor | none (nearest CR-14, bounded: the charge matches Go's backtracker) | none | The charge is real (CR-14); the open point is the valid strings refused inside the window. Carried as recorded: match long inputs with the NFA, or add the patterns and figures to doc.go and the contract request. |
| Minor 5: a schema nested deeper than 64 levels is refused as 'not valid JSON' | minor | CMP-77 | GC-19 | Unchanged. |
| Minor 6: round-8 mutants E1, G1 and F1 survive the suite | minor | E1: CMP-27, CX-23. G1 and F1: no row (nearest CX-19 and CR-25) | GC-02 (E1), GC-17 (G1, F1) | GC-02's tests add the nested-identifier shapes of E1; GC-17 takes the stateBudget delta (G1) and the equal-size reachMerger case (F1). |
| Minor 7: checkApart has no fallback finding when metaIssues returns none | minor | CMP-78, VLU-39 | GC-19 | VLU-39 adds that every fallback formats the whole error tree before the message cap. |
| Minor 8: the stack depth of in-place chains was left unverified | minor | VAL-53, CX-12 | GC-15 | VAL-53 shows the stack charge is relative to an unbounded configured limit; CR-16 adds the NFA's add() recursion (GC-11). |

Two round-9 minors (2 and 4) concern WP-51's own documentation and the accepted job-stack window rather than a library path, so no audit row covers them; they stay open as recorded and the fix pass closes them as written in the round-9 record.

## 6. Coverage

The completeness check (this run, CC rows) mapped the library and Go regexp code to the rows of sections 3.1 to 3.5. This section lists what it verified and what finalizing the document changed. Its probe files are in /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-ruralz/758d7ca3-81cf-5fbc-a967-12d97bce3d6b/scratchpad/wp51audit/complete/ (the CC rows cite them).

### 6.1 What the completeness check verified

- **Library functions.** All 309 functions of the non-test files and kind/kind.go map to a row by name or by the lines the row cites (cover.txt), with three exceptions. (1) Six functions are never reached or cost a constant, so they need no row: Compiler.RegisterFormat (compiler.go:75; c.formats stays empty and is read only when format is asserted, objcompiler.go:194), Compiler.RegisterContentEncoding and RegisterContentMediaType (compiler.go:85, :93; read only under AssertContent, which WP-51 never sets), Draft.String (draft.go:22; nothing WP-51 reaches calls it), joinSubschemas (draft.go:355; runs once while the package variables initialize, as CMP-29) and MetaSchemaMismatchError.Error (roots.go:284; the type is never constructed). (2) The format.go:42-708 validators and LocalizableError (util.go:452-467) were covered only by VLU-41 and section 4.4, both claiming they never run on bodies; that holds on walked routes only, so they now have rows CC-01 to CC-04. (3) Functions mapped by name only, which existing rows do cover: ValidatorContext methods (VAL-41, never active), queue.append, at and len (CMP-01), the objCompiler value helpers (section 2.3; CMP-49, CMP-57), root and resource clone and newResource (CMP-09), Types.Add, add, contains and typeFromString (CMP-57, VAL-05, VAL-06), uneval.isEmpty (VAL-39, VAL-43), kind/kind.go quote, joinQuoted, display and localizedError (VLU-37), and the library error types (CMP-42, CMP-43, CMP-45, REFS-46, VLU-39).
- **Keywords.** Every keyword, path and URL string in objcompiler.go and draft.go maps to sections 2.1 and 2.2, except the format-assertion vocabulary gate (objcompiler.go:443), now CC-01 to CC-04, and the draft-04 id keyword and the older-draft $ref sibling handling, which apply only under older dialects; the inspector's DraftVersion backstop catches those only after the library has compiled (CC-05).
- **Older drafts.** An older-draft dialect at a library-only position brings in that draft's meta-schema, whose uniqueItems on enum reaches the writeHash nil-Rat panic (CC-05); hash collisions among composite list entries make the meta-check quadratic there, held by MaxSchemaBytes to about 0.3 to 0.5 s (CC-06).
- **Go regexp entry points.** Every entry point maps to rows; the only uncovered one was the request-time compile of body strings:

| Entry point | Call site | Rows |
|---|---|---|
| regexp/syntax.Parse | patternsize.go:105 (compilePattern) and :163 (walk), regexcost.go:289 | CR-01 to CR-07, CR-26, CR-27 |
| regexp/syntax.Parse of a Unicode escape alone | patternsize.go:437 | CR-02; bounded: all about 1,700 valid escape texts parse in about 4 ms together, and the result is cached per document |
| regexp/syntax Simplify and Compile | regexcost.go:290 | CR-07, CR-09, CR-10 |
| syntax.Prog.StartCond, syntax.Inst.MatchRune, unicode.SimpleFold | regexcost.go:417, :457, :584 | CR-21 |
| regexp.Compile | patternsize.go:108; the library's own package-init compile | CR-08, CR-11, CR-12, CR-24, CR-28; CMP-29 |
| Regexp.MatchString | patternsize.go:94, work.go:504, states.go:576, and the meta-schema's own patterns | CR-13 to CR-23, CX-29 |
| compilePattern on body strings at request time | an asserted format 'regex' (objcompiler.go:188-192, validator.go:136-142, schema.go:393-397) | CC-01, CC-02, CC-03 (previously uncovered) |

### 6.2 What finalizing changed in this document

- Six rows added, CC-01 to CC-06 (section 3.6). CC-01 to CC-04 form the new class GC-21; CC-05 and CC-06 join GC-02, the class of positions the library compiles without the walk's rules, and also need GC-01 for the $recursiveRef route.
- Five rows reclassified, because their claim (format is never asserted, or the compiled Schema is never mutated) holds at walked positions only. Each row's evidence keeps the verifier's text and records the change; all five join GC-21:

| Row | Was | Now | Contradicted by |
|---|---|---|---|
| VLU-41 | bounded, none | partial, major | CC-01, CC-04 |
| CMP-58 | bounded, none | partial, major | CC-01, CC-03 |
| VAL-13 | not-applicable, none | partial, major | CC-01, CC-03 |
| CX-28 | not-applicable, none | partial, major | CC-01 to CC-04 (format part only) |
| VAL-68 | bounded, none | partial, blocker | CC-01 (the fatal concurrent map write) |

- Notes added in section 4.3 for two rows whose status stands: CMP-30 (vocabulary handling; its reasoning holds at walked positions only, its S cost stays not applicable) and CR-24 (the regexp cache is also reached at request time). VLU-41's former note there (an optional regex row) moved into GC-21's tests.
- Corrected: the format rows and the introduction of section 2.1, the $schema, $vocabulary and meta-schema rows of section 2.2, the format validator, regexp engine hook and concurrency rows of section 2.3, the regexp.Compile row of section 2.4, and section 4.4's statement that format validators never run on bodies.
- Re-checked when finalizing: the cited library and WP-51 lines (objcompiler.go:185-200 and :436-445, validator.go:136-142, compiler.go:321-328, util.go:451-467, schema.go:383-403, working.go:440 and :632, inspect.go:169), and TestCCBundledFormatAssertion (probe4.test, load 0.05), which again ended its child process with 'fatal error: concurrent map read and map write'.

### 6.3 What the completeness check did not cover

- Re-verification of the five areas' rows: its report states no re-run of them; it mapped library code to them and added rows where none applied.
- The leads of section 4.4: its report does not take them up.
- The Go 1.26.8 floor: its probes were built with go1.27.1 (go version of probe\*.test).

## Appendix B. Verifier summaries

The verifiers' own summaries, kept for their corrections to the auditors. Their gap groupings are superseded by section 4. The values, cross and completeness check summaries are condensed. Status counts in them are those before the completeness check (section 1.6 has the final counts).

### Compile (CMP)

I checked the auditor's 63 compile-area rows against the library (compiler, objcompiler, draft, vocab and content files, the 2020-12 meta-schemas, and the roots, root, position, util and loader files) and against WP-51. I re-ran the high-risk probes and mutants, and probed each new path. The corrected list has 79 rows: 16 bounded, 3 not-applicable, 25 partial, 35 gap. 21 are blockers and 9 major. No repository file was changed.

**Confirmed with my own re-runs**
- **Long member names (CMP-02):** a 5,000-byte name over 4,000 leaves compiles in 7.0 s.
- **Index aliases (CMP-06):** 100 aliases take 4.5 s and 1.93 GB, and the schema is accepted.
- **Long $id × references (CMP-11):** 60 KB × 2,000 refs takes 3.8 s and 1.2 GB.
- **Raw decode before the size bound (CMP-16):** 12 MB of raw input takes 1.7 s and 543 MB before the refusal.
- **$recursiveRef (CMP-33 to 35):** it panics, bypasses the number bound, and makes the loader receive a URL.
- **Invalid-$id determinism (CMP-43):** 3 different findings in 30 runs.
- **Kept annotations (CMP-55):** 12 MB kept. **Integer wrap (CMP-56)** also confirmed.
- **Untested rules:** the multipleOf, exclusiveMinimum, minContains, inert-$schema, contentSchema and anyOf mutants all survive the compile tests.

**Corrections to the auditor's rows**
- **CMP-05 is a blocker, not major.** The library rebuilds a pointer for every value the meta-schema applies '#meta' to, schema or not. The multiplier is that value count, not the schema-position count. allOf of 60,000 numbers under a 100 KB name takes 2 min 17 s and 76.9 GB before the refusal.
- **CMP-38's evidence was wrong.** The library does meta-check $recursiveRef targets. Only targets below additionalItems escape the check.
- **Thirteen rows go from bounded to partial**, because aliases, $recursiveRef or the new base mismatch (CMP-68 to 71) bypass their bound: CMP-09, 10, 18, 21 to 26, 31, 39, 42, 45, 51, 52.
- **The auditor's class fix is incomplete.** Its proposed budget (location bytes summed over schema positions) would miss most of the new members below.

**New blockers**
- **CMP-69/70 (base mismatch):** a JSON Pointer reference into a non-keyword member of an embedded resource is walked under the wrong base. The library then compiles a position the walk treated as inert. A 275-byte schema passes Check and Factory.Build, then Handle panics with 'division by zero'. The same path bypasses the number and pattern bounds. CMP-68 shows the same root cause reaching the loader, and CMP-71 shows valid schemas refused.
- **CMP-72/73:** the walk builds a pointer for every enum/const element. A 220 KB schema with a 100 KB name takes 4.7 s and 6.4 GB, and is accepted.
- **CMP-74/75:** on the refusal path, metaIssues builds a pointer per error leaf. A 220 KB type list takes 55 s and 9.7 GB.
- **CMP-76:** the library's collect builds a pointer for every item of every subschema keyword.
- **CMP-64/65:** references through an $id to a long target pointer: 3,700 references take 2.5 s and 3.5 GB, and the schema is accepted.
- **CMP-66:** a long base with 1,000 nested resources takes 13.2 s, growing quadratically.

**New minors**
- **CMP-77** (r9, still open): a schema nested deeper than 64 levels is refused with a misleading message.
- **CMP-78** (r9, still open): checkApart has no fallback finding.

**Proposed class fixes**
- **One budget** for every pointer or URL built: per value (schema or not), per reference (base, value and target pointer), and per resource ($id length × resources and positions). Plus canonical index tokens.
- **No eager pointers in WP-51:** build pointers only for the findings it reports.
- **One shared list of reference keywords**, including $recursiveRef.
- **The walk resolves a target in the library's resource**, or such pointer references are refused (the owner decides).
- **An inspector backstop:** refuse any compiled schema whose pointer the walk did not walk.

The probes are in /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-ruralz/0aabfe6e-34a6-51d2-9b9b-23886b703760/scratchpad/wp51audit/compile-verify.

### Validate (VAL)

I checked the auditor's 64 rows for validator.go and util.go against the library source (v6.0.3) and the post-fix8 WP-51 working tree. For 20 charges I mutated the code with go test -overlay and ran the cited tests. Because of the content restriction in this session, I did not run hostile-input probes (stack depth, dynamic-anchor timing, $recursiveRef shapes). Those rows rest on code reading, which shows the cited lines unchanged, and on the round-9 records.

Changes to the auditor's classification:
(1) VAL-57 moves from partial to bounded. The last case of TestHandleLocationWalk (endingContext live:2, 300 failing leaves) reaches the walk's stop function. Changing stop() to false at validate.go:125 fails that test.
(2) VAL-27 moves from bounded to partial. The sortUnits term (working.go:1290-1292) survived every cited test. In TestWorkCharges 'uniqueItems objects' the sort term cancels out, because that row compares an 808-byte name with an 8-byte one.
(3) VAL-28's test is TestWorkingSetCharges (working_test.go:733-736), not TestMatcherReach. The mutation confirms this.
(4) VAL-49's class is wider than multipleOf 0. Every keyword value that only a $recursiveRef reaches skips the walk's rules and the meta-check, and then reaches the validator. This includes const and enum numbers outside the number bounds. The library re-parses them at every comparison (util.go:326-327, exponents up to 1e6), while compareWork caps the charge at MaxNumberExponent.

New rows:
- VAL-65: the decision pass's three wrapper scopes are missing from the frame count of the scope walk.
- VAL-66: propertyNames errors alias the instance location. Not applicable, because WP-51 reads only schema locations.
- VAL-67: the uniqueItems hash seed is random. Bounded.
- VAL-68: validators run concurrently over a shared Schema. Bounded.
- VAL-69: the per-evaluation garbage from error kinds that the decision pass builds before discarding them. Bounded.

Mutations killed by the cited tests: cycle check, $dynamicRef scope term, string copy, dupItemBytes, matched-item lists (kept and listed), propertyNames wrappers, the dependencies keyword name, the not keyword name, pairwise comparisons, rationals, quoWork, lookedUpBytes, listedNameBytes.
Mutations that survived: the RecursiveRef keyword counts (VAL-47) and sortUnits (VAL-27).

Open items:
- Blockers: VAL-46 (anchor length not charged in the $dynamicRef scope walk) and VAL-49 (targets of $recursiveRef bypass the walk).
- Majors: VAL-53 (no absolute stack ceiling) and VAL-44 (load-dependent peak test).
- Minors: VAL-10, 24, 27, 28, 33, 47, 48, 59, 65.

Mutation overlays are in /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-ruralz/0aabfe6e-34a6-51d2-9b9b-23886b703760/scratchpad/wp51audit/validate-verify/mut/. No repository file was changed.

### References and resources (REFS)

I verified all 46 rows from the refs auditor. I re-ran their probes on the working tree, added my own, and ran full-suite mutants. The final list has 53 rows. No repository file changed; probes and mutants are under \<scratch>/wp51audit/refsv/.

**Confirmed gaps (re-run, all still reproduce):**
- **Base-URI mismatch** (REFS-01..05): the 345-byte panic, the bypassed number and pattern bounds, the loader being asked, the additionalItems multipleOf 0 panic in Validate, and the core-only dialect.
- **Late $id** (REFS-06..08): the 313-byte panic. It also fires when the $id sits deeper inside a target's keywords.
- **$recursiveRef** (REFS-10..13, 15): the panic also fires when the $recursiveRef is nested under properties. A missing anchor gives a raw library message at "". `$recursiveAnchor` comes back Undecided for every body.
- **Ordering** (REFS-09, 14, 37, 39): loader asked at the root, nondeterministic first finding, unparseable $id skipped silently, inert anchors and $ids accepted.
- **Dynamic-anchor charge** (REFS-29): a 100,000-byte anchor is charged the same 5,758,792 units as a 1-byte one, but runs at 126 ns/unit instead of 2.2.
- **Long locations** (REFS-18..20): a 37 KB document with one 20,000-byte name compiles in 5.6 s, allocates 653 MB and keeps about 100 MB. 20,000 leading zeros in an index take 1.27 s and 260 MB.
- **Uncounted non-schema values** (REFS-22): 1.18 s and 280 MB.

**Reclassified after mutation runs:**
- REFS-17 (vocabulary-subset $schema): bounded to partial. A mutant that lets `meta/core` through survives the whole suite.
- REFS-25 (bundled meta-schemas): bounded to partial. A $recursiveRef reaches a bundled meta-schema, and a library-only $schema reaches `meta/core`. The inspector's $recursiveRef edge mutant survives.
- REFS-31, 32, 33: bounded to partial. Every $recursiveRef mutant survives the suite: the in-place edge, the dynamic flag, the error unit and the inspector edge.
- REFS-42: not-applicable to partial (major). Dropping `definitions` or `contentSchema` from the walk's keyword list passes the whole suite, yet each lets a 250-byte panic through.
- REFS-46: the reasoning is corrected, since a custom `$vocabulary` is reachable without the loader (REFS-49).

**Confirmed bounded by mutation:** REFS-23 and REFS-28 (only the remote-reference test catches dropping $dynamicRef), REFS-30 (killed by TestEvaluationBound), and the $dynamicRef error unit in REFS-33 (killed by TestWorkCharges).

**Partial with no test:** REFS-36 (opaque-base mutant survives), REFS-21 (a root $id adds a resource scan to every reference), REFS-43 (a reserved policy.json $id gets a garbled message).

**New rows:**
- REFS-47: an ancestor target's $id causes the base-mismatch panic, in both site orders.
- REFS-48: index spellings 00, +0 and -0 under a resource flip the validation result.
- REFS-49: a $schema naming the policy document itself is served without the loader, and the document's `$vocabulary` turns off assertions.
- REFS-50: 20,000 dependencies name lists are uncounted and compile in 0.26 s.
- REFS-51: 4,000 dynamic anchors add about 0.18 s.
- REFS-52 and REFS-53: not-applicable (the "baseres is nil" panic is unreachable; implicitly compiled resource roots are already walked).

**Fix for the whole class:**
- Resolve bases by pointer prefix over every collected resource, computed to a fixpoint independent of site order.
- Treat $recursiveRef as a reference.
- Refuse non-canonical index spellings, and bound location bytes.
- Backstops: refuse any compiled position the walk did not walk live, and recover a library panic into RZ-CFG-005.
- Add a test per keyword and per reference kind, with fuzz properties for no loader call, positions subset and deterministic issues.

### Values (VLU)

Checked all 49 auditor rows for the values area (library util.go, output.go, format.go, kind/kind.go and their call sites) and added 7 the auditor missed: 56 rows, 30 bounded, 11 partial, 12 gaps, 3 not-applicable; severity 7 blocker, 7 major, 9 minor, 33 none. Method: 55 mutants, each against the full CGO_ENABLED=0 package suite with go test -overlay, and 13 probe tests, all in scratch wp51audit/verify-values/; no repository file changed. New rows: VLU-50 (wrapped negative maxLength aliases the -1 'absent' sentinel: 8.95 MB held, 104 B charged; Handle peaks at 146 MB for an 80 KB body), VLU-51 (CompileSchema panics for multipleOf 1e9999999 or 21-literal type/required lists behind $recursiveRef, in Check and Build without recover), VLU-52 (compile cost of literals at $recursiveRef targets: 0.42 s and 56 MB for 459 bytes), VLU-54 (URL resolution per reference: 200 KB schema with a 100 KB $id and 3,500 $refs compiles in 6.3 s and 3.26 GB), VLU-55 (kept memory for long names: 20 to 41 MB for a 13 KB document), VLU-56 (hash-pass parse charge untested). Corrections to the auditor: VLU-02's 0.93 to 1.43 ns/unit was not reproduced (0.17 to 0.64 ns/unit), the row stays partial because the exponent term has no test; VLU-03, 21, 28, 32, 33, 35, 38, 41 and 42 are now backed by killed mutants; VLU-10 is bounded twice over (two redundant conditions).

### Cross (regexp and interactions) (CR, CX)

Checked the auditor's 55 rows for the cross area and added 3: 58 rows, 23 bounded, 1 not-applicable, 22 partial, 12 gap. 48 overlay mutants (scratch wp51audit/verify-cross/mutate.py): 43 killed, 4 survived, so 6 rows moved from bounded to partial; no repository file modified. Moved to partial: CR-04 (no test fails with the (?i) Perl/POSIX fold charge at 0), CR-21 (no test fails without queueBound's per-queue scan charge), CX-25 (the contains per-loop list charge), CX-21, CX-22 and CX-27 (hold only outside $recursiveRef targets; blocker with CX-02). CR-02 raised to major ((?i)\p{Ll} appends 1,336 ranges, 143 charged; 8 such patterns compile in 550 ms against 105 ms for the benchmarked worst). CX-03 lowered to major (the refusing loader still blocks every fetch; the defects are the loader call and the finding's pointer). Test names corrected for CR-13, CX-08 and CX-22. Reconfirmed: CR-01 ("[" + "[:"x130,000 + "a]" accepted after 7.80 s), CR-08 (999 nested captures refused with the meta-schema's message), CR-11 (385 patterns keep 11.4 MB against doc.go's 6 MB), CR-15 (2,055-instruction pattern reserves 205,851 B and peaks at 655,360 B), CR-16 (2,700-branch alternation grows the stack by 512 KiB uncharged), CX-02 (4,000x over the pattern bound accepted under $recursiveRef), CX-06, CX-17 (4,000-byte name over 4,000 positions 2.57 s), CX-13 (TestHandleUnevaluatedBranches failed in 2 of 3 concurrent runs). New rows: CR-28 (expression text kept outside the measure), CX-29 (meta-schema patterns and formats on schema strings), CX-30 (4,000 dynamic anchors add about 0.1 s of quadratic compile work).

### Completeness check (CC)

The completeness check mapped every library function (309 in the non-test files plus kind/kind.go), every keyword string in objcompiler.go and draft.go and every Go regexp entry point to the rows. Almost all of it maps to existing rows. The exception is format assertion, which a resource's $schema can switch on along library-only routes: the format 'regex' check then calls WP-51's compile-time regexpCache (schema.go:383-403, a plain map) at request time, so parallel requests crash the process with a fatal concurrent map write (3 of 3 runs), memory grows with each distinct body string, and the request-time work is uncharged (CC-01 to CC-04). An older-draft dialect at a library-only position also brings in that draft's meta-schema, whose uniqueItems on enum reaches the writeHash panic class (CC-05), and hash collisions among composite list entries in the meta-check are quadratic there (CC-06). It proposed GC-21 (format and content keywords are annotations on every route: the inspector refuses any compiled Format or content field, the regexp engine is frozen after compile, GC-01 and GC-02 close the routes), CC-05 and CC-06 for GC-01 and GC-02, and the reclassification of VLU-41 and the correction of section 4.4. No repository file was changed.
